## Slice kernels — DTW kernels, variants, lower bounds, distance surface
Summary: This slice is the DTW kernels, their variants, the lower bounds and the single-pair distance API: about 7.1k library lines, plus roughly 6k lines of tests that exist only because of it.

The core is healthy. There are three templated kernels (linear, banded and EAP) driven by Cost and Cell policies, the costs are compile-time, scratch space is thread_local, and decode_pair is the one pair decoder for host and device. The variants themselves (DDTW, WDTW, ADTW, Soft-DTW, MSM, TWE, AROW/ZeroCost/Interpolate for missing data, DTW_I for multivariate) are sound and should stay.

The bloat is around that core, in five places:
- (a) The pruned matrix fill: 566 lines, plus the LowerBoundStrategy enum and routing code in Problem, Python and MATLAB. It can never skip a pair of an exact matrix. The project's own baseline proves it does at least as much work as brute force, yet Auto and Python's `use_pruning=True` still select it by default.
- (b) About 70% of lower_bound_impl.hpp (Kim, Enhanced, Webb, the multivariate and squared variants) plus the lower_bounds.hpp trait table and distance_metric.hpp. Only that fill uses them. TADPole needs only the envelope and symmetric LB_Keogh.
- (c) Three resolvers for variant × missing strategy, plus the test-only core::dtw_distance and dtw_runtime with DTWOptions and ConstraintType.
- (d) About 22 copies of the orient/empty/identity/band preamble, a legacy (a,b) cost-functor family beside the Span*Cost family, and dead debug overloads.
- (e) Validation repeated in layers: an enum-membership switch runs in about 36 per-pair wrappers, variant parameters are re-checked per pair, there are f32 "representability" checks, a Soft-DTW subnormal-gamma fallback, and 1.2k lines of tests that cast invalid integers to enums.

One real defect: Soft-DTW distances use the full O(n·m) matrix kernel, a grow-only thread_local buffer of about 512 MB per thread at 8K samples.

- **kernels-06** [yagni-abstraction; high; -110/+10 LOC; user_visible=false; v1.0.0=no; perf=low; verifier=modified] dtwc/core/dtw_dispatch.cpp:163-227,275-281 (make_wdtw_f64/make_wdtw_f32/make_wdtw); dtwc/Problem.cpp:381-411 (refresh_variant_caches); dtwc/Problem.hpp:152,233,678-683 (wdtw_weights_cache_); dtwc/warping_wdtw.hpp:137-153 (detail::cached_wdtw_weights)
  What: There are three WDTW weight mechanisms. (1) Problem's unordered_map cache keyed by max_dev, pre-populated serially for every series length. (2) The thread_local single-entry cache in warping_wdtw.hpp. (3) The f64 miss path, which allocates a fresh weight vector per pair. On top of this, f64 and f32 have separate make_wdtw functions because the map holds only data_t. The thread_local cache alone is lock-free and already serves f32 and the facade.
  Evidence: The map lookup and miss-allocation are at dtw_dispatch.cpp:176-185 and 196-202. f32 uses wdtwBanded(..., band, g) → cached_wdtw_weights (dtw_dispatch.cpp:213-226, warping_wdtw.hpp:137-153). refresh_variant_caches runs on every rebind (Problem.cpp:430). Building weights costs O(max_len) exp calls, against O(n·m) for the DP, so recomputing on a thread-local miss costs at most about 1/min_len relative, even on variable-length data.
  Action: Collapse to one mechanism without the regression. At bind time, build the per-length weight table once, serially, in the target precision T (this covers f32 too). Hold it by value (or shared_ptr<const>) inside the resolver closure, and delete Problem::wdtw_weights_cache_, wdtw_weights_cache(), refresh_variant_caches() and the separate make_wdtw_f64/f32. On a miss (a DBA centroid of new length), fall back to the thread_local cache, and make that cache reuse its buffer (compute into cached_w in place) instead of assigning a new vector. Existing WDTW tests must stay digit-identical. Measure one variable-length banded WDTW fill before and after. [MODIFIED by verifier; original: Replace make_wdtw_f64, make_wdtw_f32 and make_wdtw with one `make_wdtw<T>` that calls `wdtwBanded<T>(x, y, band, g)` or `wdtwBanded_mv<T>(..., band, g)`; these already use the thread_local cache. Dele]
  Verifier: There are indeed three WDTW weight mechanisms (dtw_dispatch.cpp:165-227, Problem.cpp:381-411, warping_wdtw.hpp:147-160), and the Problem-held map is what couples the resolver to Problem. The perf justification is wrong for the banded case, though. The auditor compares O(max_len) exp calls with an O(n·m) DP, but WDTW is normally banded: the DP costs O(n·(2b+1)) cells, while wdtw_weights still evaluates max_len exp values and cached_wdtw_weights assigns a freshly returned vector on every miss (a heap allocation). On variable-length data the single-entry thread_local cache misses on almost every pair, because max(len_i, len_j) changes along a row. With a narrow band (b≈5-10), the exp calls plus the allocation are the same order as the DP, a slowdown of order 1× rather than '1/min_len'. It also puts an allocation into the hot loop, which the design.md §9 thread_local-scratch invariant forbids. Equal-length data would be unaffected. Not in v1.0.0.

The independent-MV and univariate rules are checked in distance_semantics.hpp:81-104 and again in make_independent, make_msm, make_twe, make_soft_dtw and make_interpolate (dtw_dispatch.cpp:65,259,291-297,318-338). require_metric_supported repeats in its own words the rule Problem applies at Problem.cpp:581-591.   What: Soft-DTW carries a special numeric path, based on frexp/scalbn, for subnormal gamma values where 1/gamma overflows to inf. A smoothing parameter below 2.2e-308 is not a real use case; rejecting it takes one line.

That means >= DBL_MIN in validate(DistanceConfig), plus float(gamma) >= FLT_MIN in the f32 bind check kept under kernels-07 (a typed InvalidInput). Then store only inv_gamma in SoftCell and in the gradient, delete SoftGammaScale and the two-branch softmin_gamma_unchecked, and turn the denormal-gamma test cases into rejection cases.   Verifier: SoftGammaScale (dtw_kernel.hpp:86-110) and the two-branch softmin exist only for subnormal gamma, where 1/gamma overflows. Requiring gamma >= numeric_limits<double>::min() makes 1/gamma finite in double (1/DBL_MIN ≈ 4.5e307 < DBL_MAX), so the fallback can go for f64. The finding ignores the f32 path. resolve_dtw_fn<float> casts gamma to float (dtw_dispatch.cpp:268), and a double gamma in [DBL_MIN, FLT_MIN) becomes a float subnormal or zero, so the float inv_gamma overflows to inf. Removing the fallback without a float-precision bound would make f32 Soft-DTW return NaN (0*inf at delta=0) or inf, which is silent: NaN means 'uncomputed'. Combined with kernels-07's plain static_cast, this becomes a silent wrong answer.

But Problem.set_distance_matrix in Python (_dtwcpp_core.cpp:901-914) copies a user ndarray into the dense matrix with no finite check. The same applies to the mutable C++ dense_distance_matrix() and to MATLAB's matrix setter, and a mapped cache is opened from disk. Deleting them turns that into a silent +inf or NaN objective. The checks in fast_pam.cpp:207 sit inside a try within the omp region, so they are not UB. The volatile-accumulator question (a cross-route byte contract under -fassociative-math) needs the test the auditor names before any change.

Verifier found missed items:
- **kernels-M1** scripts/check_docs_contract.py:1127-1450 (assert_lb_enhanced_webb_derivation_sync); :689-1126 (assert_lb_keogh_derivation_sync, trim); docs/derivations/03-lb-enhanced-webb.md (764 lines); docs/derivations/README.md index entry; docs/derivations/02-envelopes-lb-keogh.md pruned-route sections (:391-405,:492); tests/unit/adversarial/test_lb_keogh_derivation.cpp squared/MV/pruned cases (:206-208,:391-393,:457) (-1250): The gate and derivation documents that pin the LB_Enhanced/Webb/Kim/squared/MV lower bounds and the pruned route. The D3 function requires pruned_*.{hpp,cpp}, enums/LowerBoundStrategy.hpp and the 03 derivation. The D2 function pins lb_kim_valid and `sum += excess * excess;`, which exists only in lb_keogh_squared. → Delete the D3 gate function and docs/derivations/03-lb-enhanced-webb.md (plus its README index entry). Trim the D2 gate function to Keogh and envelope markers only. Trim the pruned and squared sections of derivation 02 and the squared, MV and pruned cases of test_lb_keogh_derivation.cpp.
- **kernels-M2** python/dtwcpp/__init__.py:237 (_valid_metrics); python/src/_dtwcpp_core.cpp:1175-1235 (hand-rolled OpenMP loop with per-thread exception slots in compute_distance_matrix) (-70): Python's compute_distance_matrix carries its own metric whitelist and its own parallel all-pairs loop, with error slots, beside the Problem fill. It is a second matrix engine that also skips EAP for band<0 in both its branches. → Once kernels-01 lands, make compute_distance_matrix build a Problem (or call the core fill) and delete the private loop and the whitelist. Parse the metric through names.hpp (kernels-10).
Keep (looks like bloat, earns its place):
- core::dtw_kernel_linear / dtw_kernel_banded / dtw_kernel_eap with Cost and Cell as template parameters, plus StandardCell/ADTWCell/SoftCell/AROWCell: These are the single place where the recurrence lives, with compile-time costs and no per-cell indirection (design.md §9). Every variant builds on them.
- The EAPruned kernel (dtw_kernel_eap) and its relaxed threshold: It is the one exact-matrix DTW work reduction that actually saves cells, and the default for unbanded Standard in Problem. The x16 slack protects exactness under -fassociative-math (ledger C-16).
- thread_local grow-only scratch in the kernels, MSM/TWE, DDTW and DTW_I: It keeps the parallel fill free of heap allocation after warm-up (a design invariant).
- dtw_band_bounds with overflow-safe clamps: It costs a few instructions per row, is correct for any size_t, and has no int narrowing. This is not a count guard.
- detail/decode_pair.hpp (host/CUDA and the MSL string): It is the single pair decoder, shared by CPU MPI, CUDA and Metal, and it fixed real overflow and FP32 bugs. It is a design invariant.
- core/public_distance.hpp normalize_public_distance: It maps the float32 no-path sentinel FLT_MAX to DBL_MAX for the f32 CPU path and both GPU backends. That is correctness at the double API boundary, in 32 lines.
- detail::require_finite at the checked boundary (distance::*, soft_dtw_gradient, the fill pre-scan), run once per call or per fill: NaN means 'missing' or 'uncomputed' in this library, so a single O(n) scan at the boundary is what keeps the unchecked per-pair layer sound.
- The variants DDTW, WDTW, ADTW, Soft-DTW (+gradient), MSM, TWE; missing strategies ZeroCost, AROW, Interpolate; multivariate dependent and independent (DTW_I): They are user-facing features and look correct on inspection. soft_dtw_gradient is used by barycenter and Python.
- compute_envelopes (Lemire O(n)), Envelope, lb_keogh, lb_keogh_symmetric: TADPole uses them to actually skip pairs, which is admissible there, and the benchmark uses them too.
- v1.0.0 names dtwFull, dtwFull_L, dtwBanded (vector overloads): They shipped in v1.0.0, so they stay as thin forwards even after the wrappers collapse.
- The distance:: vector overloads beside the span overloads: Template deduction does not convert a vector to span<const T>, so without them `dtw(vx, vy)` does not compile for C++ users.
- The float32 compute path (resolve_dtw_fn<float>, dtw_fn_f32_): The f32 Parquet and OneBatchPAM paths rely on it. Only its representability checks are bloat (kernels-07).
- z_normalize (two-pass, stable): It is small, correct and used by the Python and MATLAB bindings. The MSVC #if around omp simd is minor noise.
- core::ClusteringResult: A plain data struct shared by every algorithm.
- The unchecked (warping*.hpp) vs checked (distance::*) layering itself: It is the right split for performance. The problem is only that the unchecked layer re-validates anyway (kernels-05).
Silent bugs reported:
- dtwc/soft_dtw.hpp:144; dtwc/core/dtw_dispatch.cpp:271; dtwc/core/dtw_kernel.hpp:220-221; dtwc/core/scratch_matrix.hpp:65-74: Not a wrong answer but a scalability defect. Every Soft-DTW distance, in the Problem fill and in the facade, allocates an O(n·m) thread_local full DP matrix that only ever grows: about 512 MB per thread at 8K samples, never released. Users with long series can run out of memory with nothing indicating Soft-DTW as the cause. See kernels-08 for the fix (use the linear kernel). (dtw_dispatch.cpp:270-272 calls dtw_kernel_full<T, SpanL1Cost<T>, SoftCell<T>>. dtw_kernel.hpp:220 holds `thread_local core::ScratchMatrix<T> C; C.resize(n_short, n_long)`, and ScratchMatrix::resize reallocates only upward (scratch_matrix.hpp:65-74).)
- dtwc/Problem.cpp:1189-1193; python/src/_dtwcpp_core.cpp:1202-1206: Not wrong values but a silently slower default. Auto (N>=64, band>=0, Standard/ADTW, L1) chooses the pruned fill, which does at least brute-force work plus recomputation. Python's compute_distance_matrix, with its default use_pruning=True and band=-1, runs dtwFull_L with early abandon through the standalone pruned copy, not the exact EAP kernel that the Problem path uses. Users on the default settings pay extra DTW work. (.claude/baselines/2026-07-08-lb-cascade.md proves 'work(pruned) >= work(brute)'. pruned_distance_matrix.cpp:280-298 recomputes after an abandon. The Problem path uses EAP for unbanded Standard (dtw_dispatch.cpp:139-140).)
- dtwc/distance.hpp:44-72; dtwc/core/dtw_dispatch.cpp:120-122 (comment), make_zero_cost/make_arow/make_ddtw: The two entry points disagree on which metrics a configuration accepts. distance::missing, distance::arow and distance::ddtw (and the dtw_runtime facade) honour SquaredL2, while a Problem with the same configuration refuses any metric other than L1 for DDTW and for every missing strategy (its make_* functions hard-code L1). This raises a typed error, so it is not a silent wrong answer, but the same request succeeds on one path and fails on the other. (distance.hpp:44-50 comment: 'Problem::set_metric is stricter: its resolver passes the metric to the Standard kernels only'. make_arow uses SpanAROWL1Cost unconditionally (dtw_dispatch.cpp:96,106). make_zero_cost calls dtwMissing_banded without a metric (:49,54).)
Verifier on bugs: 1. Soft-DTW keeps an O(n·m) matrix per thread. Real (a scalability defect, not a wrong value). dtw_dispatch.cpp:270-272 and soft_dtw.hpp:144 call dtw_kernel_full. dtw_kernel.hpp:220-221 holds a thread_local ScratchMatrix, and resize only ever grows (scratch_matrix.hpp:65-74). At 8192 samples that is about 537 MB per thread, kept for the thread's lifetime. Switching to the linear kernel is valid, because SoftCell::combine treats up and left symmetrically, but the LSE sum order changes, so results match to within ULPs and are not digit-identical; that needs the tolerance the audit proposes. soft_dtw_gradient needs its two full matrices by nature, so it keeps that footprint.

2. Auto and Python default to the slower pruned fill. Real (a silent slowdown with correct values). Problem.cpp:1189-1192 selects Pruned when pruned_strategy_applicable holds (N>=64, band>=0, Standard/ADTW, L1, f64, dense). pruned_distance_matrix.cpp:280-298 recomputes every abandoned pair in full. The baseline .claude/baselines/2026-07-08-lb-cascade.md:19 proves work(pruned) >= work(brute). Python defaults to use_pruning=True (_dtwcpp_core.cpp:1247). One correction: for band=-1 both Python CPU branches use dtwFull_L, not EAP (:1202-1225), so Python misses EAP whether or not pruning is on.

3. The facade and Problem disagree on metrics. Real, but it raises a typed error, so it is not a silent wrong answer. distance.hpp:44-72 lets DDTW and every missing strategy take SquaredL2. Problem refuses any non-L1 metric unless the variant is Standard with MissingStrategy::Error (Problem.cpp:581-591), and its make_zero_cost, make_arow and make_ddtw hard-code L1 (dtw_dispatch.cpp:43-55, 76-111, 145-161). The same configuration therefore works on one path and throws on the other. This has to be resolved before kernels-03 merges the two paths.

4. New hazards that the proposed fixes would create:
- kernels-07 plus kernels-11 on the f32 path (plain static_cast, then deleting the subnormal-gamma path) would let a double-valid gamma below FLT_MIN, or a parameter above FLT_MAX, reach the float kernels. The results would be NaN or inf distances, with NaN read as 'uncomputed', a silent wrong answer. The f32 representability check must stay, in a smaller form.
- kernels-12's premise misses Python set_distance_matrix (_dtwcpp_core.cpp:901-914), which accepts non-finite matrices unchecked.
- kernels-06 as written would put an exp-heavy weight rebuild and a heap allocation into the per-pair path for variable-length banded WDTW.
Interface notes: How a user sees this slice today:
- **C++** has four ways to compute one distance: the unchecked free functions (dtwBanded, wdtwBanded, adtwBanded, dtwMissing_banded, dtwAROW_banded, msm_distance, ...), each with pointer, span and vector overloads, often in both Full_L and Banded forms; the checked dtwc::distance::* per-variant functions; the distance::dtw(params) dispatcher; and the test-only core::dtw_distance and core::dtw_runtime(DTWOptions with ConstraintType).
- **Python** has seven per-variant functions (dtw_distance, ddtw_distance, wdtw_distance, adtw_distance, soft_dtw_distance, dtw_distance_missing, dtw_arow_distance) and none for MSM/TWE. DTWClustering builds a Problem to get one MSM/TWE or missing-data distance. Python also exports a ConstraintType it cannot use, a MetricType.L2 that no parser names, and LowerBoundStrategy/Pruned knobs that only make things slower.
- **MATLAB** has one command per variant (dtwc.distance.standard/ddtw/wdtw/adtw/soft_dtw/missing), with its own 'pruned' and lb-strategy strings.
- **The CLI** parses names case-insensitively through names.hpp, while Python and MATLAB use a stricter, separate parser (parse_metric_token) that accepts fewer names.

A nicer surface, with one vocabulary everywhere:
- **One value type:** `DistanceConfig {variant ('standard'|'ddtw'|'wdtw'|'adtw'|'softdtw'|'msm'|'twe'), params (g, penalty, gamma, c, nu, lambda), band, metric ('l1'|'squared_euclidean'), missing ('error'|'zero_cost'|'arow'|'interpolate'), mv_mode ('dependent'|'independent')}`. The Problem holds one, and set_device(cpu|gpu|hpc) stays orthogonal to it.
- **One checked single-pair call:** C++ `dtwc::distance::dtw(x, y, cfg)`, Python `dtwcpp.distance(x, y, variant=..., band=-1, metric='l1', missing='error', **params)`, MATLAB `dtwc.distance(x, y, 'variant', ...)`.
- **One resolver**, resolve_dtw_fn(cfg), behind both the single-pair call and the matrix fill, so the two paths cannot disagree.
- **One names table** (names.hpp) used by all four front ends.
- **One matrix entry point:** compute_distance_matrix calls the same fill as Problem, with EAP for unbanded Standard and no pruning flags.
- **Convenience aliases:** the per-variant functions (ddtw_distance, ...) can stay in Python as one-line aliases; in C++ the unchecked kernels stay public for speed, documented as unchecked.

Files cited are under C:\D\git\dtw-cpp\ (e.g. C:\D\git\dtw-cpp\dtwc\core\dtw_dispatch.cpp, C:\D\git\dtw-cpp\dtwc\distance.hpp, C:\D\git\dtw-cpp\dtwc\core\pruned_distance_matrix.cpp, C:\D\git\dtw-cpp\dtwc\core\lower_bound_impl.hpp, C:\D\git\dtw-cpp\dtwc\warping.hpp).

## Slice data-io — Data container, loaders, readers, persistence (mmap, checkpoint, matrix IO)
Summary: This slice covers the Data container, the text/Parquet/Arrow readers and persistence. It is about 6.3k library lines plus roughly 9k lines of tests. The core is sound and none of it needs to change: the strict text parser, the fast_float and nanoarrow ingest, the dense packed NaN matrix and Data's f64/f32/view modes. The bloat sits in persistence and storage routing, all of it 2.0-only and never released:
(1) Four on-disk formats exist for one result. The N^2 matrix is stored three ways: a v3 mmap cache with SHA-256 identity, per-row two-lane atomic XOR digests, two-phase publication and a file lease; a dense CSV checkpoint with generations, a CURRENT pointer and a SHA-256 manifest; and CSV export. A binary result checkpoint replays a finished run (--resume). The CLI already refuses to combine the CSV checkpoint with the mmap cache.
(2) A StoragePolicy auto-spill loads the whole dataset into RAM first and then copies it to a temp .dtws file, which is never deleted.
(3) A metadata-only Data mode is produced only when the C++ device is 'hpc', and nothing can compute on the result.
(4) The Parquet eager reader duplicates the chunk reader, and the Arrow IPC reader duplicates the nanoarrow ingest (and skips null checks).
(5) The usual count-overflow and saturating-arithmetic guards.
The wheels, MEX and release artefacts all build with LLFIO=OFF, so none of the mmap machinery reaches pip or MATLAB users. Roughly 9-10k lines (library + tests) can go.

- **data-io-03** [defensive-guard; high; -1550/+60 LOC; user_visible=false; v1.0.0=no; perf=low; verifier=modified] dtwc/core/mmap_distance_matrix.hpp (783 lines); dtwc/core/crc32.hpp; tests/unit/core/unit_test_mmap_distance_matrix.cpp (1371 lines)
  What: Cut the v3 mmap distance cache back to what a recompute-on-doubt cache needs: header {magic, version, N, 32-byte fingerprint} + packed doubles with NaN = uncomputed, validated at open by magic/version/fingerprint equality/exact file length. Remove the per-row two-lane XOR payload digests (avalanche64, payload_contribution, compute_row_digest, initialize_payload digest part, validate_payload, xor_digest_delta, the digest footer), the publication-state byte and two-phase barrier publication, persist_range, the exclusive file lease (acquire_session_lease, owns_session_lease_, custom move ops), the header CRC, v1/v2 migration messages, checked_layout's six overflow checks, the unreachable logic_error lock-free checks, the fingerprint-less open(path) overload, and the Dense/Mmap copies of max/count_computed/all_computed (make them one free function over raw()/packed_count()).
  Evidence: set() (lines 671-691) runs a release fence plus two atomic fetch_xor calls, each with 6 avalanche64 mixes, on every cell write in the fill loop. That is pure overhead for a file whose only failure mode is 'recompute'. Aligned 8-byte doubles cannot tear at page writeback, so after a crash a cell is either NaN (recomputed) or its exact value. The digests defend only against media corruption, which the filesystem handles. Creation writes NaN over all N(N+1)/2 cells and then calls barrier(wait_all) on the whole file (lines 638-645): for N=1e5 that is a 40 GB durable flush before any work starts. The lease uses try_lock_file, which the header itself says may fail on filesystems without whole-file locking (lines 450-458), i.e. on the HPC network filesystems this cache is for. The lock-free checks are guarded by static_asserts and commented 'unreachable' (lines 267, 632). checked_layout guards n*(n+1)/2 overflow, but any N that large already fails the file-length equality (a one-line plausibility bound such as n <= 2^32 suffices for file-derived N). The fingerprint check at open (lines 411-418) is the one guard that prevents a silent wrong answer, and it stays.
  Action: Rewrite as proposed (~250 lines), but create() must NaN-fill the payload, call barrier(wait_all) once, and only then write and persist the header magic, so that a header can never be durable over zero pages. Drop the digests, digest footer, publication-state byte, CRC, lease, custom move ops, lock-free logic_errors, the fingerprint-less open, v1/v2 messages and the checked_layout overflow checks (keep the file-length equality and an N plausibility bound). Share max/count_computed/all_computed with Dense. Record in DECISIONS that this reverses the v3 payload-authenticated format. [MODIFIED by verifier; original: Rewrite MmapDistanceMatrix at about 250 lines: create(path, n, fingerprint) truncates, maps, NaN-fills and writes the header once. open(path, expected_fingerprint) checks magic/version/N-bound/file le]
  Verifier: Right: the per-cell set() (mmap_distance_matrix.hpp:710-722) does a fence plus two atomic fetch_xor calls, each with 4 avalanche64 mixes. It is called from the fill loop (Problem.cpp:1115,1123). The lease exists only so that digest validation cannot race a writer; without digests, concurrent same-fingerprint writers store valid distances and cause no harm. The lock-free logic_errors are marked unreachable. The fingerprint-less open(path) has no production caller (Problem.cpp:840 uses the fingerprint overload). One piece must stay: the create-time ordering. truncate() gives zero pages, and 0.0 is a valid 'computed' distance. If a power loss persisted a valid header before the NaN-filled pages, reopening would silently read 0.0 distances, a silent wrong answer. The minimal safe create is therefore: NaN-fill, barrier, then write or persist the magic/header last. That keeps one barrier and a 'header last' rule, but not the publication-state byte, CRC, digests or lease. Process crashes (as opposed to OS or power loss) are safe either way, because the page cache survives. After a reopen, a creator that died mid-fill leaves no valid magic, so open() rejects the file.

- **data-io-09** [defensive-guard; high; -90/+10 LOC; user_visible=false; v1.0.0=no; perf=none; verifier=modified] dtwc/io/parquet_schema.hpp:30-43,78-83,90-93; dtwc/io/parquet_chunk_reader.hpp:204-216,245-270,278-280,303-305,384-391,401-410,504-516; dtwc/core/storage.hpp:51-70; dtwc/Problem.hpp:564,594,610
  What: Remove the remaining count/size overflow guards and programming-error throws in this slice (the others go with data-io-01/03/04/05/06): parquet_leaf_count and the INT_MAX leaf-index checks, saturating_add/saturating_multiply/saturating_from_i64 on RAM estimates, the int64 row-offset overflow check, and the std::logic_error range checks in read_row_groups*/read_rows_impl (turn them into asserts). Also remove validate_storage_policy/validate_precision on enum-class values that bindings can only produce valid (nanobind enum, MATLAB string parse).
  Evidence: size_t is 64-bit on every supported target, so byte totals of real Parquet files cannot approach 2^64, and saturation only changes behaviour in impossible cases. Parquet leaf counts beyond INT_MAX cannot occur (the Parquet C++ API itself indexes columns with int). The logic_errors are commented 'Programming error: fast_clara walks [0, num_row_groups()) in batches' (parquet_chunk_reader.hpp:278). The enum validators ('Invalid StoragePolicy value.') can only fire on a static_cast of garbage. Owner's rule of 2026-09-27: one index type, no count guards. The file-derived checks that bound pointer arithmetic stay (require_list_range, the negative-metadata check). This touches design.md §9 'RAM arithmetic saturates' and ledger B-22's DO-NOT-BREAK list, which must be amended: a plain 64-bit sum is exact here.
  Action: Apply as proposed, but keep the one-line int64 row-offset overflow check in the ParquetChunkReader constructor (or accumulate in uint64 and compare with total_rows_), because it bounds signed arithmetic on file-derived values. Keep the negative-metadata and require_list_range checks. [MODIFIED by verifier; original: Use plain size_t/int64_t arithmetic in ParquetChunkReader's estimates and delete the three saturating helpers. Delete the leaf-count overflow throws. Replace the logic_error range checks with assert. ]
  Verifier: Most of it stands. The saturating size_t helpers act on Parquet metadata, and a real file cannot come near 2^64 bytes. Wrap-around is defined for unsigned types, and a lying file then fails inside Arrow's read. Leaf counts above INT_MAX need more than 2^31 schema fields. The validate_* enum checks cannot fire from the bindings: nanobind already raises ValueError on invalid enum construction (test_invalid_distance_enums.py:25-33), and MATLAB parse_storage_policy throws on unknown strings. The logic_error range checks guard programming errors, so an assert suits them. One exception: the int64 row-offset check at parquet_chunk_reader.hpp:207 (rg_row_counts_ > INT64_MAX - offset) protects a SIGNED sum of file-derived row counts. Without it a crafted file causes signed-overflow UB before the later offset != total_rows_ check can run. That check guards file input, not a count the library produced, so it stays. design.md §9:321 ('RAM arithmetic saturates') must be amended, as the auditor says.

- **data-io-13** [other; high; -2/+2 LOC; user_visible=false; v1.0.0=yes; perf=none; verifier=confirmed] dtwc/fileOperations.hpp:463,519
  What: load_folder and load_batch_file deep-copy the whole dataset on return: `return std::pair(p_vec, p_names);` builds the pair from lvalues, so both vectors (every series) are copied, doubling peak RAM and adding a full memcpy of the dataset on every text load.
  Evidence: fileOperations.hpp:463 and :519 construct std::pair via CTAD from the named locals p_vec/p_names. Implicit move applies only to a returned id-expression, not to constructor arguments, so pair(const T1&, const T2&) copies. This is present since v1.0.0 (git show v1.0.0:dtwc/fileOperations.hpp lines 149, 220).
  Action: Change both to `return std::pair{ std::move(p_vec), std::move(p_names) };`. No test change needed beyond the existing loader tests. Optionally drop readFile's reserve(10000)+shrink_to_fit.
  Verifier: fileOperations.hpp:465 and :519 are `return std::pair(p_vec, p_names);`. CTAD with lvalue arguments picks pair's copying constructor, so both vectors (the whole dataset) are deep-copied and not moved. Implicit move applies only to a returned id-expression. The same code is in v1.0.0 (v1 fileOperations.hpp around lines 149 and 220). The fix is internal, not a surface change.
- **data-io-14** [interface-wart; medium; -40/+30 LOC; user_visible=true; v1.0.0=partly (DataLoader is v1; the rest is 2.0); perf=none; verifier=modified] dtwc/cli/run.cpp:92-232; dtwc/api.cpp:84-142; python/src/_dtwcpp_core.cpp:249-283; python/dtwcpp/_api.py:68-101

A .parquet/.arrow path in Python is therefore parsed as delimited text and fails with a numeric-field IOError instead of reading, or instead of the typed 'Parquet not built' error the CLI raises (run.cpp:135-160). The CLI's read_series (run.cpp:188-240) is the only multi-format reader. There are currently five user-facing load entry points (DataLoader, dtwc::load/Dataset, io::load_parquet_file/directory, ArrowIPCDataSource, data_from_arrow) with different naming rules.

Bind it as _read_data, adding a column keyword. C++ dtwc::cluster needs no change beyond calling the shared function inside run(). Add one Python test: load().as_data() on a .parquet file either reads it or raises dtwcpp.IOError naming the missing Arrow build. [MODIFIED by verifier; original: Lift Source/detect_input/require_input_format_is_built/read_series out of cli/run.cpp into api.cpp as dtwc::read_data (declared in api.hpp). The CLI calls it, and _read_data binds it (adding a column=]
  Verifier: Python confirmed: Dataset._as_data → _dtwcpp_core._read_data → text DataLoader (_dtwcpp_core.cpp:249-264, _api.py:83-86), so dtwcpp.load('x.parquet') is parsed as CSV. C++ Tier-1 is already consistent, though: dtwc::cluster(Dataset path) sets config.input and calls run(config) (api.cpp:277-285), which goes through the CLI's classify/read_series. The mismatch is therefore Python versus C++/CLI, plus direct DataLoader users. Wheels build with Arrow OFF, so in practice Python gains the typed 'Parquet not built' IOError instead of a numeric parse error. That is still the right fix.
Keep (looks like bloat, earns its place):
- dtwc/extern/fast_float + io/parse_number.{hpp,cpp}: Floating-point std::from_chars only compiles at a macOS 26 deployment target (parse_number.hpp:7-10). fast_float (Apache/MIT/BSL) is the locale-free, correctly rounded replacement. It is included privately in one TU and is used by the text loader and the CSV matrix reader.
- dtwc/extern/nanoarrow + io/arrow_c_data.{hpp,cpp}: This lets Python ingest polars/pyarrow/DuckDB/pandas through the PyCapsule protocol with no pyarrow and no Arrow C++ dependency (always built, one TU, Apache-2.0). Arrow C++ remains an optional extra for Parquet/IPC files. The two do not overlap: nanoarrow cannot read Parquet, and Arrow C++ is too heavy to require. data-io-08 makes nanoarrow the single Arrow→Data converter.
- core/portable_random.hpp (189 lines): std::uniform_int_distribution/std::shuffle are implementation-defined, so the same seed gives different medoid inits on libstdc++, libc++ and MSVC (Linux, macOS and Windows wheels). All six functions are used (portable_real_below/unit_interval via portable_weighted_index). DECISIONS.md rejects PCG/xoshiro for the same reason.
- core/sha256.hpp: This is the distance-cache/checkpoint fingerprint over data + configuration. It must be stable across hosts (std::hash is salted/implementation-defined), and it is NIST-vector tested. It stays after data-io-03/04 as the single identity. DECISIONS.md rejected xxHash.
- Fingerprint check when opening an mmap cache/checkpoint: Without it, a cache built for other data or another band/metric would be silently reused, which is a silent wrong answer. Keep it as a single equality check at open (data-io-03 removes everything around it).
- Data heap-f32 mode and view mode: f32 halves resident memory and matches GPU FP32 and FastCLARA streaming. View mode lets CLARA/CLARANS subsample without copying. design.md 2026-09-23 decided against templating Data. Only the metadata-only mode goes (data-io-02).
- DenseDistanceMatrix (packed, NaN = uncomputed, no atomics) and the distMat_t variant: This is a design §9 invariant, and the variant is FROZEN in the 2.0 contract. It stays minimal, apart from sharing max/count_computed/all_computed with Mmap.
- ParquetChunkReader metadata-first planning and read_rows sparse access: This is the real large-N (HPC) data path: FastCLARA streams row groups under a RAM budget without materialising 100M series. Only the f32 twins and saturating arithmetic go (data-io-07/09).
- Text-parser strictness in fileOperations.hpp (BOM without seek, blank-line error, one-value-per-line in folder files, locale-free ASCII helpers, sorted_directory_files, UTF-8 path helpers, explicit delimiter wins over extension): Each rule fixes a documented silent-wrong-input bug (shifted values, index column clustered, per-machine file order, mojibake names). They are cheap and run once per load.
- llfio as the mmap backend (D-18): This is the owner's decision (prefer a mature library for mapping/flush/lock corners). After data-io-03 only create/open/truncate/map/barrier are used. Note that no shipped artefact enables it (wheels, MEX and release all set LLFIO=OFF), so either test a wheel with it ON or accept that mmap is a source-build feature.
- Problem_IO.cpp 1.x writers (write_clusters, write_silhouettes, writeMedoids, writeBestRep, write_medoid_members, read/write_distance_matrix CSV): They are the v1.0.0 surface (Problem_IO.cpp shipped in v1.0.0), and the owner said the output names are fine. Only their open/close helpers are deduplicated.
- LoadOptions + positional load_folder/load_batch_file overloads: The positional form is the v1.0.0 signature, and LoadOptions is the single internal carrier. Keeping both costs two one-line forwarders.
Silent bugs reported:
- dtwc/io/arrow_ipc_reader.hpp:95-147; dtwc/cli/run.cpp:222-230: The Arrow IPC reader never checks nulls. A null list cell or a null value slot is read as whatever bytes the values buffer holds (usually 0.0) and flows into the DTW distances. The Parquet and nanoarrow paths reject nulls with InvalidInput. (grep 'null_count\|IsNull' dtwc/io/arrow_ipc_reader.hpp returns 0 hits. series() returns raw spans over flat_values_ (lines 199-205), and the CLI copies them verbatim. Compare parquet_schema.hpp:113-121 (require_no_nulls) and arrow_c_data.cpp:103-116. Fixed by data-io-08.)
- dtwc/DataLoader.hpp:125-144,208-217; dtwc/core/mmap_data_store.hpp:160-178: StoragePolicy::Auto (the default for Problem::set_data and the CLI on cpu) spills to a temp file 'dtwc_store_<hex>_<n>.dtws' in temp_directory_path that is never deleted. Every spilled run leaks a file larger than half the free RAM. (MmapDataStore::create opens with llfio flag::none (no unlink_on_first_close). grep remove/unlink in mmap_data_store.hpp gives 0 hits, and nothing in DataLoader/Problem deletes the path. Fixed by data-io-01.)
- dtwc/io/arrow_c_data.cpp:140-181: data_from_arrow_stream (the __arrow_c_stream__ path used by polars/pandas) discards the struct's utf8 name column and renames every series 'series_<i>', while the single-array path (__arrow_c_array__) keeps the names. The same DataFrame gets different names depending on which protocol the producer exposes. (The stream loop calls data_from_arrow(&schema, &batch, {}) and keeps only d.p_vec (lines 166-169), then synthesises names (174-177). Ledger B-09 records this as open.)
- dtwc/checkpoint.cpp:626-667; dtwc/cli/run.cpp:657-662: load_checkpoint returns false for 'absent', 'identity mismatch', 'corrupt' and 'any exception' alike. The CLI then recomputes every distance and exits 0, and says so only under --verbose. A checkpoint the user pointed at but that belongs to other data or settings is silently ignored, which breaks the no-silent-fallback rule (the answer is still correct, but the user loses hours). (try { ... if (... identity mismatch) return false; ... } catch (...) { return false; } (checkpoint.cpp:629-666). run.cpp:658-661 prints 'No valid checkpoint found ... starting fresh' only if config.verbose. Fix inside data-io-04: return false only when the file is absent, and throw InvalidInput on mismatch or IOError on corruption.)
- dtwc/io/parquet_reader.hpp:127,146-150; dtwc/io/parquet_chunk_reader.hpp:116: The same scalar-column Parquet file is named differently by the two readers: eager load_parquet_file uses path.stem().string() (native narrow encoding, so non-ASCII names become mojibake on Windows, unlike path_to_utf8 elsewhere), and the streaming reader uses 'series_<offset>'. labels.csv from an eager run and from a streamed run of the same input therefore disagree. load_parquet_directory also calls ::tolower on a plain char (UB for negative values) and does not skip dot-files (AppleDouble '._x.parquet'). (Lines cited. fileOperations.hpp:381-391 (sorted_directory_files) and :48-52 (path_to_utf8) are the loaders' own rules. Fixed by data-io-07.)
- dtwc/cli/run.cpp:208-215: A .dtws '.names' sidecar with fewer lines than N silently leaves the remaining series names empty, which then appear as empty names in labels.csv. (The loop stops at getline failure and leaves names[i] default-constructed, with no count check. Moot if data-io-06 deletes .dtws.)
Verifier on bugs: (1) Arrow IPC reader nulls: REAL. arrow_ipc_reader.hpp has no null_count or IsNull check (lines 95-147), and series() returns raw spans that run.cpp:224-229 copies verbatim. Nuance: a null list cell usually has equal offsets, so it becomes an empty series, which Problem::set_data then rejects as 'empty' (a misleading message, but not silent). A null VALUE slot inside a list is read as whatever is in the buffer, and that is silent. The Parquet (require_no_nulls) and nanoarrow (arrow_c_data.cpp:103-116) paths reject both kinds.
(2) Temp .dtws leak from StoragePolicy::Auto: REAL, in builds with llfio. The CMake default is DTWC_ENABLE_LLFIO=ON (Dependencies.cmake:192), and dtwc_cl on cpu sets Auto (cli/run.cpp:579). MmapDataStore::create opens with flag::none (mmap_data_store.hpp:176), and nothing in DataLoader, Problem or the store removes the file. It does not reach wheels, MEX or release binaries, which all set LLFIO=OFF.
(3) data_from_arrow_stream drops struct names: REAL. arrow_c_data.cpp:166-177 keeps only d.p_vec from each batch and then synthesises series_<i>. The single-array path keeps utf8 names (lines 109-120). This is ledger B-09.
(4) load_checkpoint returns false for mismatch or corruption and the CLI starts fresh silently: REAL. checkpoint.cpp:629-666 returns false on N or identity mismatch, on a malformed file and from catch(...). run.cpp:657-661 reports it only with --verbose, and the end-of-run save_checkpoint (run.cpp:752-757) then publishes a new generation that replaces the user's other checkpoint. api-contract §2.7:514 documents the false return, so this is a documented silent fallback that violates the no-silent-fallback rule, not a wrong answer.
(5) Parquet naming: PARTLY REAL. The eager-versus-streamed labels disagreement for a scalar column cannot happen, because streaming rejects scalar columns (fast_clara.cpp:468-472). Real: path.stem().string() (parquet_reader.hpp:127) garbles non-ASCII stems on Windows, where the loaders use path_to_utf8. std::transform(..., ::tolower) on plain char (line ~150) is UB for negative bytes. Dot-files such as '._x.parquet' are not skipped. Additional real bug the auditor missed: in load_parquet_directory, each list-layout file restarts its names at series_0 (load_parquet_file names by vecs.size()-1 within the file). A directory of list-layout files therefore yields duplicate series names in labels.csv.
(6) A .dtws .names sidecar with fewer than N lines leaves empty names: REAL. In run.cpp:212 the loop stops at the first getline failure and leaves names[i] empty. Extra lines are also ignored silently. This goes away if data-io-06 is taken.
Interface notes: How a user sees this slice today:
- **C++** has five ways to get series into a Problem: DataLoader (v1 builder; now also storage_policy/ram_limit/mmap_cache_path/load/load_local/load_stored/load_metadata/count), dtwc::load → Dataset, io::load_parquet_file/directory, io::ArrowIPCDataSource, and io::data_from_arrow. Only the CLI dispatches by file extension.
- **Python**'s dtwcpp.load(path) reaches only the text DataLoader, so a .parquet or .arrow path fails as bad CSV instead of reading or raising a typed 'not built' error.
- **MATLAB** alone exposes set_storage_policy.
- **Persistence** is exposed four ways under different names:
  - use_mmap_distance_matrix(path) — the auto-resuming cache;
  - save_checkpoint/load_checkpoint(prob, dir) + CheckpointOptions — dense CSV generations, mutually exclusive with the cache;
  - write/read_distance_matrix — CSV;
  - save/load_binary_checkpoint + CLI --resume — which replays a finished result.
- **'ram_limit'** means three different things (DataLoader spill threshold, Problem::set_ram_limit, CLI --ram-limit Parquet budget).
- **Shipped builds:** wheels, MEX and release binaries are all built without llfio, so none of the mmap/.dtws/StoragePolicy::Mmap surface works for pip or MATLAB users. They get typed IOErrors.

A nicer surface, all achievable by the findings above:
1. **Reading:** one reader, dtwc::read_data(path, skip_cols, skip_rows, delimiter, column), used by the CLI, C++ dtwc::load and Python dtwcpp.load, dispatching text/folder/Parquet/Arrow by extension with a typed error when a format is not compiled in. DataLoader stays as the v1 text builder (load() only).
2. **Data** has three modes: heap f64, heap f32 and view.
3. **The N^2 matrix** has one binary file format:
   - use_mmap_distance_matrix(path) maps it;
   - save_checkpoint/load_checkpoint read and write it for a heap matrix;
   - CheckpointOptions.save_interval means 'sync/save every k rows' for both;
   - CSV stays purely as interchange (v1 read/write_distance_matrix).
4. **Results** persist only as the CSV artefacts, written by one shared writer for the CLI and Result.save.
5. **'hpc'** stays a Python/CLI submission concept (the path is forwarded) with no C++ Data mode behind it.

## Slice session-surface — Problem session, Tier-1 api, CLI/Config/run, base layer, enums, forwarding headers
Summary: Scope: Problem (806 + 1731 lines), Tier-1 api (414), CLI/Config/run (1760), base/ (about 1,400), enums, types, forwarders. The CLI layer is in decent shape: one Config, one key table in cli::bind(), and api.cpp is a thin wrapper over run(Config). Most of the bloat is in Problem.

1. **Drift detection in Problem.** A distance-semantics "drift detection" layer exists only because 2.0 made variant_params, missing_strategy, distance_strategy and cuda_settings public fields, and Python hands them out by reference. That layer is about 450 lines plus about 400 test lines. Every O(1) dist_by_ind lookup runs two full semantic preflights, a 12-field snapshot compare and a relocation check. The code comment claims one preflight, but there are two.
2. **Pruned default.** fill_distance_matrix still picks Auto → Pruned. LESSONS:160 proves Pruned always does more work than BruteForce, and DECISIONS C-05 (2026-09-07) already approved removing it from Auto.
3. **C++ hpc path does nothing.** It checks .env, runs an ssh probe and does a metadata-only load, and every consumer then refuses it. Python never calls it.
4. **Defensive-guard classes.** Enum validators that nothing can trigger (a 1,172-line test pins them), INT_MAX and overflow guards, logic_error checks, and a 100-line exact-rational `--ram-limit` parser.
5. **Duplicate method dispatch.** Methods are dispatched three times: Problem::cluster, run(), and Python _run_local_method. Output writers exist three times, and one copy (Result::save) has an out-of-bounds read.

Roughly 7–9k lines can go (about half of it tests) without touching a performance invariant.

  What: The default Auto distance strategy picks Pruned (pruned_strategy_applicable, Problem.cpp:948-963), which does strictly more work than BruteForce on an exact matrix. When a lower bound exceeds the nearest-neighbour threshold, the pair is computed with early abandon, and an abandoned pair is recomputed in full. So every pair costs one DTW or more, never zero, plus the bound and envelope overhead. LowerBoundStrategy (7 values, 'for diagnostics and cross-comparison' per its own header), the LB_Enhanced/LB_Webb bounds (used only here), the standalone compute_distance_matrix_pruned copy and Python's compute_distance_matrix(use_pruning=True) default all serve this pessimisation. On top of that sit five silent downgrade branches in fill_distance_matrix (missing strategy, mmap, checkpoint, f32 throws, metric/ndim). Once Pruned goes, DistanceMatrixStrategy is {Auto, BruteForce, CUDA, Metal}, which just restates Device.

MSVC builds with /openmp:experimental (dtwc/CMakeLists.txt:184), which accepts a signed 64-bit loop variable, so an index_t loop is fine.

Two parts of the action are unsafe or oversized:
(1) 'At 32-bit third-party boundaries (HiGHS HighsInt, Gurobi int, MPI counts, CUDA grid dims) use a plain static_cast … never throw'. A static_cast of N^2 > 2^31 into HighsInt, an MPI int count or a CUDA grid dimension silently wraps and builds a wrong model or launch: a silent-wrong answer. design.md:299-300 names mip/index_guard.hpp as the one legitimate check. Keep those guards, or chunk where we own the loop.
(2) Migrating index_t through clusters_ind/centroids_ind (v1 public vector<int>), ClusteringResult, every algorithm signature, Python and MATLAB is a cross-slice API change. It is far larger than 60/15 LOC and needs design.md §9 rewritten (line 297 currently says counts are int). The owner's 2026-09-27 instruction does authorise it, but it should be scheduled as its own wave.

(3) /we4061 fires on every switch that has a default but does not list every enumerator, which would break many existing switches. Use /we4062 only, the MSVC equivalent of -Wswitch.

The proposed 20-line double parser has two problems.
(1) `static_cast<size_t>(std::llround(v*mult))` is UB when v*mult exceeds the long long range, and llround caps at 2^63. One range check is needed, and it is not defensive bloat: it avoids UB.
(2) Config promises that to_config_text → parse_config round-trips (config.hpp:122-124). ram_limit is written back as an integer byte count, and a double parse loses integers above 2^53. That is unrealistic as RAM, but a round-trip test could hit it.

The Python aliases n_repetition, set_number_of_clusters, distance_matrix_numpy, set_distance_matrix_from_numpy, the *_index scores, normalized_mutual_information, get_device, ClusterResult and Result.medoid_indices never shipped, and neither did the _F22_DEPRECATION_POLICY handshake. (Assumed: no 2.0 pre-release reached PyPI. Check before deleting.) Update api-contract §4 and docs/content/api/tier-2.md alias tables.
Keep (looks like bloat, earns its place):
- dtwc/detail/decode_pair.hpp (host/device pair decoder plus the MSL string): design §9 invariant: the single pair decoder shared by CUDA, Metal, MPI and CPU. The 'defensive' clamps protect the N<2 case and seed-correction loops that fixed a real retired bug.
- base/names.hpp Name tables with parse_name/name_of (one table per enum, canonical name first): 81 lines, and the only string↔enum mechanism shared by the CLI, config files, MEX and Config text round-trips. It deletes per-front-end parsers. Trim aliases, not the mechanism.
- cli::bind() as the single Config key table, plus to_config_text/device_text: 'every setting of a run is a Config field' is what makes hpc submission 'serialise Config, run dtwc_cl' (design §6). parse_config should gain its production caller (session-surface-07), not be deleted.
- cli/config_file.hpp (fkYAML → CLI11 ConfigItem adapter): The owner wants TOML and YAML through CLI11 with flags winning. The adapter is small, and the null-is-not-empty rule prevents a silent `band: ~` → 0.
- run.cpp pre-I/O checks: require_input_format_is_built (under #ifndef), require_input_options_apply, validate_gpu_request before reading series, refuse_gpu_method, configure_distance_storage refusals: No-silent-fallback rule: a flag that cannot be honoured is a typed error before any work. The #ifndef placement is the LESSONS F9 fix (a Parquet file would otherwise be parsed as CSV).
- validate_fill_request: band feasibility (gap > band) and the non-finite/NaN scan, run once per fill: Prevents silent wrong answers: banded kernels return the finite max() sentinel, so clustering would sum 1.8e308 (D-12, FX-15). It must run once in the builder, never per lookup (see -01).
- SHA-256 fingerprint of data+semantics at use_mmap_distance_matrix bind/open, and checkpoint identity: Durable caches are reopened across runs. Without it, a SquaredL2 matrix would be reused for L1 or for different data. Keep the bind-time check, drop the per-lookup re-check.
- run_openmp exception capture (the capture itself): thread_local scratch growth can throw bad_alloc inside a parallel region, and an escaping exception is UB/std::terminate. Only the lowest-index ordering is surplus.
- The single-thread warning (one message, once): Owner rule: no silent single-core execution. Simplify the apparatus, keep the behaviour.
- dtwc.hpp #error under __FINITE_MATH_ONLY__, and -fno-finite-math-only on the target: NaN marks missing values and uncomputed entries. Under -ffinite-math-only these tests fold away and results are silently wrong (design §9 FP-model invariant).
- close_output checking the stream after close (one copy): A full disk or a quota fails writes after a successful open, and without the check a truncated file sits behind exit 0 (B-05). Deduplicate it, but keep it.
- std::variant<DenseDistanceMatrix, MmapDistanceMatrix> in Problem, and mmap_threshold: HPC scale: the O(N^2) matrix must be able to live on disk, and the visit costs nothing next to a DTW.
- Problem::set_solver returning bool, with run() turning false into SolverError: The bool return is v1.0.0 surface. The Tier-1/CLI path is already a typed error, so there is no silent fallback there.
- api.hpp Tier-1 cluster(load(...)) → run(Config), and Result: A thin wrapper (about 20 lines of logic). MATLAB's tier1_cluster and examples/cpp/quickstart use it, and it gives C++ users the device→load→cluster flow the owner likes.
- The 21 Problem and 2 DataLoader [[deprecated]] shims whose names really are in v1.0.0: 'People use this library.' Each is a one-line forwarder, kept one cycle (B-19).
- base/system_memory available_ram_bytes: Drives StoragePolicy::Auto spill-to-mmap. Small and platform-isolated (keeps <windows.h> out of headers).
- root parallelisation.hpp / settings.hpp / timing.hpp / utility.hpp forwarders: These paths shipped in v1.0.0.
Silent bugs reported:
- dtwc/api.cpp:195-203 (Result::save), dtwc/cli/run.cpp:519-530,812-815,836-839: Result::save on a Result from a RAM-capped streamed Parquet run reads out of bounds. On that route run() never calls prob.set_data, so the Problem holds no series, but it sets prob.clusters_ind = result.labels (N entries). Result::save then loops over labels().size() and calls problem_->series_name(i), which reads data_.p_names[i] on an empty vector: UB, garbage or a crash in Release. Result::distance_matrix() on the same Result silently returns an empty vector while labels() has N entries. run.cpp's own writer handles this case (output_series_name with streamed_count); the duplicated writer in api.cpp does not. (Read run.cpp:519-530 (stream_payload skips set_data), 812-815 (clusters_ind = result.labels), api.cpp:195-203 (series_name(i) for each label), Data.hpp:79-83 (name(i) indexes p_names without a check). Reachable via C++ dtwc::run(config) with a list-per-row Parquet input, method clara/auto and ram_limit below the estimate, followed by result.save(dir). Fixed by session-surface-08 (one writer carrying streamed_count).)
- dtwc/Problem.cpp:948-963,1188-1195: This is a silent performance regression, not a wrong answer. Auto still resolves to the Pruned strategy for Standard/ADTW L1 univariate data with N>=64 and band>=0, although DECISIONS C-05 (approved 2026-09-07) set Auto = BruteForce and LESSONS:160 proves Pruned does strictly more work on an exact matrix. So every default banded CPU fill pays the bound, envelope and early-abandon overhead, plus a full recompute of each abandoned pair. (Read pruned_strategy_applicable and the Auto resolution in fill_distance_matrix. DECISIONS.md:117. LESSONS.md:160. pruned_distance_matrix.cpp:275-290 (abandon → recompute).)
- dtwc/Problem.cpp:872-877,764-772: dist_by_ind's comment claims 'Exactly ONE preflight per call', but validate_mmap_cache_identity(), called on the next line, starts with preflight_current_distance_semantics() again. Every O(1) lookup in the FastPAM SWAP / CLARANS / Benders loops runs two full semantic validations. Results are unaffected; this is a performance problem with a false comment. Fixed by session-surface-01. (Read Problem.cpp:872-877 and 764-772.)
Verifier on bugs: 1. Result::save out-of-bounds read on a streamed-Parquet Result: REAL, inferred from code and not executed.
- run.cpp:580-597 calls prob.set_data only when !stream_payload.
- run.cpp:748 sets prob.clusters_ind = result.labels (N entries).
- Result::labels() returns problem_->labels() (api.cpp:158).
- Result::save loops over labels().size() and calls problem_->series_name(i) (api.cpp:216-217, and 224-225 for medoids). series_name maps to Data::name(i), which indexes p_names[i] unchecked (Data.hpp:79-83) on an empty vector: UB.
- Reachable only through the C++ `dtwc::run(config)` with a list-per-row Parquet file, method clara or auto, and ram_limit below the estimate (plan_parquet_load, run.cpp:820-849), followed by result.save(dir).
- Not reachable through dtwc::cluster/Tier-1 (ram_limit 0), Python (run is not bound) or MATLAB (it goes through dtwc::cluster).
- run.cpp's own writer handles the case through output_series_name(streamed_count).
- Result::distance_matrix() on such a Result calls fill_distance_matrix on N=0 and returns an empty vector, which is inconsistent with N labels.

2. Auto → Pruned silent performance regression: REAL.
- Problem.cpp:1187-1192 resolves Auto to Pruned whenever pruned_strategy_applicable holds.
- pruned_distance_matrix.cpp:275-290 recomputes every abandoned pair with dtw_with_abandon(-1).
- LESSONS.md:160 records that pruned work >= brute-force work.
- DECISIONS.md:117 C-05 (Auto = BruteForce) is approved but not implemented.
- Results are exact; the cost is time only.

3. Double preflight in dist_by_ind: REAL.
- Problem.cpp:873-874 claims 'Exactly ONE preflight per call', then line 875 calls preflight_current_distance_semantics() and line 876 calls validate_mmap_cache_identity(), whose first statement (Problem.cpp:775) is preflight_current_distance_semantics() again.
- This affects performance only, not results. The overhead was not measured.

Additional adjacent performance issue (observed): is_distance_matrix_filled() (Problem.hpp:684-690) does an O(N^2) all_computed scan and is called once per Lloyd assign_clusters iteration (Problem.cpp:1498).
Interface notes: What a user sees today:

**C++**
- Three entry points:
  - Tier-2 `Problem`: about 90 public members, 22 deprecated shims.
  - Tier-1 `dtwc::cluster(dtwc::load(...), k, method, band, device, max_iter)`.
  - `dtwc::run(Config)`.
- Device can be set four ways: `dtwc::device()` (process-wide, which Problem deliberately ignores), `Problem::set_device`, `set_distance_strategy(CUDA/Metal)` and `set_cuda_settings`.
- Two method enums: `Method` (4 values, Problem) and `ClusterMethod` (9 values, run).
- GPU precision is an int (0/1/2) and Benders is a free string.
- `LowerBoundStrategy` (7 values) and `DistanceMatrixStrategy` (5 values) expose internal optimisation hints. One of them (Pruned) is a pessimisation.

**Python**
- Tier-1 `dtwcpp.cluster()` re-implements the method dispatch and the auto rule instead of calling C++ `run()`.
- `device('hpc')` is handled purely in Python, while C++ Env has an unused `.env`/ssh copy.
- `Problem` properties hand out nested structs by reference, which is why C++ carries drift detection.
- There are three capability reports: `system_check`, `system_info` and `test.*`.

**MATLAB**
- Routes through `dtwc::cluster`, which is good.
- Also carries a "legacy" stateless `cluster` command that was never released, `test_*` probes, and writes `prob.band` raw.

**CLI**
- Lean: one Config, one key table, TOML/YAML through CLI11.
- Alias sprawl: 9 GPU-precision spellings, 9 Benders spellings, method aliases, `--restart`.

A nicer surface, the same in every language:
1. `dtwc::device("cpu" | "gpu[:N]")` once. `hpc` exists only in Python/CLI submission and raises a typed error elsewhere.
2. `Config` is the only run description: CLI flags, TOML/YAML, Python kwargs (via `parse_config`), MATLAB name-value pairs.
3. `dtwc::run(config[, data]) -> Result`, where `Result` has labels, medoids, cost, iterations, converged, `score(name)`, `save(dir)` and `distance_matrix()`. Python and MATLAB bind exactly this.
4. `Problem` stays as the Tier-2 session. Distance semantics are private, behind setters (`set_band`, `set_variant`, `set_metric`, `set_missing_strategy`, `set_device`). There is one `Method` enum with all 9 algorithms, and `cluster()` dispatches them all.
5. One `index_t` (int64 by default) for counts, labels and medoids, with no count guards.
6. One `system_check()` that reports threads and GPU, including a validated tiny kernel.

Beyond this slice:
- There are three checkpoint mechanisms: dense CSV `--checkpoint`, the auto-resuming mmap cache, and the binary result `--resume`. Worth a unification look in the checkpoint slice.
- The `.dtws` and Arrow IPC readers in run.cpp copy the mapped series into `vector<vector>`, doubling memory. A mapped series store is the later step the code already notes.

## Slice algorithms-mip — Clustering algorithms, initialisation, scores, MIP / exact solvers
Summary: The slice is 7,044 lines in dtwc/algorithms and dtwc/mip, plus 807 in initialisation and scores. The core is sound: FastPAM1 with the ΔTD decomposition, CLARA with a Parquet streaming route, TADPole, OneBatchPAM, LR-core (subgradient/Kelley + reduced-cost fixing + B&B) and the compact HiGHS/Gurobi model. Around it sit four layers of bloat.
(1) Research scaffolding shipped as public API. PDLP is an LP "arbiter" that was measured as dominated. CLARANS is marked "experimental" and was never benchmarked. Benders is a second large-N exact route next to LR-core, which the design names as the large-N route. FastPAM1Naive and FasterPAM have no production caller.
(2) Defensive layers. INT_MAX count guards and index_guard.hpp. A finite check on every distance lookup inside the SWAP loops, with the try/catch/exception_ptr/critical scaffolding that goes with it. Validators for out-of-range enum values plus logic_error defaults. A snapshot/validate/rollback transaction around the MIP results.
(3) Duplication. K-means++ appears four times, plus "_seeded" twins. The Problem write-back is copied eight times. The p-median model is built three ways.
(4) Deprecated aliases for names that were never released.
I estimate about 4.5–5k lines can go from the library, tests, bindings and docs with no user who can lose anything. Everything cut here is 2.0-only, except the v1 init::random/Kmeanspp and silhouette signatures, which stay.

It must stay an explicit compact solve (no silent rerouting to LRCore), and it makes the backend index check from -06 reachable.   What: The medoid loops re-check every distance lookup for finiteness, and wrap every OpenMP loop in try/catch, exception_ptr, a first-failure index and a named critical section. This runs in the hottest loops: find_best_swap does O(N) per candidate and O(N²) per sweep. The checks guard a condition the builder already excludes. OrderedMedoidObjective adds throwing and a 'became non-finite' check to what should be a plain ordered sum.
  Evidence: fast_pam.cpp:238-240 (find_best_swap) and :206-207 call require_finite_candidate_distance on every lookup. There are 22 call sites of require_finite_*/OrderedMedoidObjective (git grep across dtwc). Problem::validate_fill_request (Problem.cpp:981-1000) already rejects the one real non-finite source, an infeasible band. Non-finite input values are rejected when the getter binds (one_batch_pam.cpp:92-96 comment). design.md §9 says 'guards live in the builder (throwing inside an OpenMP region is UB)'.
  Action: Remove require_finite_medoid_distance and require_finite_candidate_distance from all scans, and have each algorithm check isfinite(total) once and throw InvalidInput. Delete the try/catch/exception_ptr/critical scaffolding only in loops that read a filled matrix or precomputed table: fast_pam compute_nearest_and_second, find_best_swap and update_removal_loss, the k==1 scan, and the OneBatchPAM nearest scans. Keep one shared capture helper (replacing the duplicated per-file capture_assignment_failure and critical blocks) around the loops that invoke the DTW function: CLARA in-memory and chunked assignment, and the OneBatchPAM table fill. Reduce OrderedMedoidObjective to a non-throwing volatile ordered accumulator with add(double), add(span) and value(), and keep it for the chunked streaming sum. Trim the non-finite injection tests to match. [MODIFIED by verifier; original: Remove require_finite_medoid_distance and require_finite_candidate_distance from every scan: fast_pam, fast_clara in-memory and chunked, one_batch_pam and Problem Lloyd. With nothing left to throw ins]
  Verifier: The per-lookup finite checks are removable. In fast_pam the matrix is filled first (fast_pam.cpp:547), and finite inputs and band feasibility are rejected in validate_fill_request. Inf that comes from overflow only yields NaN deltas, which fail every `<`, so no swap is taken and a final isfinite(total) check catches it. The try/catch cannot simply go everywhere, though. Several of these OMP loops call the DTW function itself, not a matrix lookup: fast_clara assign_all_points_direct (:163-189, `distance(point, ...)`), the chunked Parquet assignment (:289-317, `dtw_fn(...)`), and the OneBatchPAM table fill (one_batch_pam.cpp:105-135). The DTW function can throw: it grows thread_local scratch (bad_alloc), and distance.hpp:167 throws InvalidInput for unsupported combinations. An exception leaving an OMP region is UB (design §9). Chunked Parquet chunks are also never validated by validate_fill_request, because the parent Problem is metadata-only. Problem::assign_clusters runs through dtwc::run, which already captures exceptions. OrderedMedoidObjective is also used as a cross-chunk streaming accumulator (fast_clara.cpp:273, :318), so a span-only ordered_sum is not enough: keep a small accumulator class. The LOC saving is closer to 350-400 than 550.

  Verifier: Each validate_* has only its own algorithm's entry as caller. There are no static_casts to these enums in dtwc/, python/ or bindings/. nanobind enums and the name tables (CLI and MATLAB) cannot produce out-of-range values. None of these is v1. One mechanical catch: switches in value-returning functions (for example compute_barycenter at barycenter.cpp:516) need something after the switch once `default: throw` goes. Without it, -Wreturn-type fires and falling off the end is UB. Use C++23 std::unreachable() where available, or keep a single terminal throw after the switch, never a `default:` label, so -Wswitch keeps working. validate_pam_variant and validate_assignment_matrix_layout disappear with -05 and -07 anyway. The distance_sampling_weights logic_error (selected index out of range) is fine to drop. Its finite checks, however, also guard D-sampling weights for Soft-DTW's negative values, and they belong to -09's helper.

(1) The frozen api-contract pins the unseeded behaviour (docs/api-contract-2.0.md:197-200, tier-1.md:111): 'The unseeded Tier-2 fast_pam overload intentionally retains its legacy mutable std::mt19937 engine ... use fast_pam_seeded'. The MATLAB mex relies on this (dtwc_mex.cpp:1293-1298), as does Python `fast_pam`. Merging fast_pam and fast_pam_seeded therefore changes a contract line and needs a DECISIONS.md entry plus a changed MATLAB omitted-seed path. (2) Forwarding the v1 one-argument init::Kmeanspp and random through a seed drawn from randGenerator changes their output sequence for a given engine state. The signature is kept, but the behaviour changes and must be recorded in CHANGELOG. (3) The 'all weights zero' fallback (identical series) cannot be deleted. It must live inside the single helper, or k-medoids++ cannot return k distinct indices. barycenter's copy uses D² over align_squared on sequences, not Problem indices, and skips the negative-translation step, so the shared template must take the weight function as a parameter. Production users of fast_pam_seeded that must switch: cli/run.cpp:676,679; fast_clara.cpp:378,496,548; warm_start.cpp:23; python _api.py:461, _clustering.py:392 and sklearn.py:223.

  What: The HiGHS compact model is assembled as int Triplets, then std::sort(N² log N²) with a comparator misleadingly named RowMajor (it sorts column-major), then converted to CSC. pdlp_lp.cpp builds the same p-median matrix directly row-wise. Of solver_types.hpp's ten declarations, only Triplet and RowMajor are used.

Setting integrality makes it the MIP; leaving it off gives the LP, which the test from algorithms-mip-02 can reuse. Delete solver_types.hpp and the triplet and sort code.

The model's column and row numbering is closed-form, so a direct CSC or row-wise build gives an identical HighsLp, and the solver path and conformance digits are unchanged provided the column index order (Nb*i+j, FacilityMajor) is preserved. Building column-wise directly also avoids HiGHS's internal row-to-column transpose. dtwc/types/types.hpp:14 has a comment pointing at solver_types.hpp that should be updated. v1 had Triplet and RowMajor in types/, but only as internals: v1's public surface did not name them for users.

On tied optima it can change which optimal medoid set is published. That is not a wrong answer, but it breaks the 'no-op means digit-identical conformance output' rule unless it is checked.
