Verifier found missed items:
- **algorithms-mip-M1** bindings/matlab/+dtwc/{davies_bouldin_index,dunn_index,calinski_harabasz_index,adjusted_rand_index,normalized_mutual_information}.m, bindings/matlab/dtwc_mex.cpp (old score command names), bindings/matlab/+dtwc/{davies_bouldin,dunn,calinski_harabasz,adjusted_rand,normalized_mutual_info}.m (-150): The MATLAB deprecated score aliases (never released; MATLAB did not exist in v1.0.0), and the canonical wrappers that still dispatch through the old mex command names. The audit's -14 covers only C++ and Python. → Delete the five alias .m files, rename the mex commands to their canonical names, update the canonical .m files and the MATLAB tests that call the aliases (folded into the -14 modification).
Keep (looks like bloat, earns its place):
- FastPAM1 ΔTD decomposition (fast_pam.cpp find_best_swap, update_removal_loss, fastpam1_swap_impl), parallel over candidates, with find_best_swap itself sequential: Measured 2.95–8.06× over the naive swap (baseline 2026-07-08-faster-pam-bench.md). Parallelising find_best_swap was measured at about 10× slower (DECISIONS). This is the default route for every PAM, CLARA and warm start.
- The fast_pam k==1 special case: Without it, second_dist=+inf makes ρ and the corrections ±inf, the result is NaN, and the BUILD medoid comes back instead of the optimum. The CLARA median oracle caught this. The case can shrink once algorithms-mip-04 removes its try/catch, but it must stay.
- Point-ordered objective summation (the volatile fold in OrderedMedoidObjective): The build enables -fassociative-math, and the published objective is compared digit-for-digit across routes and in conformance. Keep it as a plain ordered_sum without the throwing (algorithms-mip-04).
- LR-core: subgradient root, Kelley root (HiGHS), reduced-cost fixing with its 1e-9-scaled tolerance, and the y-branching B&B: This is the only exact route that scales and needs no solver (the subgradient path runs without HiGHS), per design.md §10. The fixing tolerance prevents fixing an optimal tied medoid out. The need<0 check guards an inconsistent LB/UB pair.
- nearest_medoid<Dist> header template: It is zero-overhead, has several callers in LR-core (and Benders until it goes), and pins one tie rule.
- set_highs_option (the throwing variant) in highs_support.hpp: A rejected HiGHS option would otherwise quietly run the solver on its defaults (the no-silent-fallback rule). It has 3+ callers.
- HiGHS/Gurobi kOptimal/GRB_OPTIMAL status checks and the SolverError when LR-core reaches the node cap uncertified: Without them an exact entry point publishes a heuristic or garbage result as optimal (audit finding #7; LESSONS 'An exact entry point that runs out of iterations must throw').
- CLARA Parquet chunked assignment (assign_all_points_chunked<F32>): This is the HPC/out-of-core route for the 100M-series target. The compile-time F32 template keeps the precision branch out of the inner loop.
- TADPole (density peaks with LB/UB pruning): It is the only consumer that can use lower-bound pair-skipping: 78% pruning was measured and the result was identical to brute force (LESSONS:25). It is wired into Method::TADPole.
- OneBatchPAM (the NNIW estimator): It is the O(N·m)-memory, matrix-free k-medoids behind the Python and sklearn 'onebatch' route. Only its extra weighting modes and the warning apparatus go (algorithms-mip-11).
- Barycenter k-means with DBA, SSG and Soft-DTW: It is a user-facing, sequence-centroid method (the tslearn TimeSeriesKMeans equivalent) exposed in Python. The three averaging methods cost about 150 lines together. Trim the scaffolding (algorithms-mip-12), not the methods.
- Lloyd k-medoids (Problem::cluster_by_kmedoids_lloyd) and the init::random/init::Kmeanspp one-argument signatures: These are v1.0.0 API (Method::Kmedoids, init_fun); people use them.
- scores: the seven indices and cluster_counts_checked/require_two_realised: Real metrics, and the realised-cluster guard fixes a genuine vacuous-pass bug (declared but empty clusters). silhouette(Problem&) is v1 API.
- The compact Balinski model on HiGHS and on Gurobi with the FastPAM warm start: Method::MIP is v1.0.0. Gurobi's branch priority on the diagonal is a real speed-up. Only the builder duplication goes (algorithms-mip-10).
- fast_clara_plan's sample-size policy (resolve_clara_plan without the int guard): The CLI uses it to decide streaming and GPU refusal before loading data (cli/run.cpp:517-525).
Silent bugs reported:
- dtwc/Problem.cpp:1436-1461: With N>200, Method::MIP ignores the selected Solver. A user who sets Solver::Gurobi silently gets HiGHS Benders, and a Gurobi-only build throws 'Benders decomposition requires HiGHS' for a request it could honour. This breaks the no-silent-fallback rule. It is fixed for free by algorithms-mip-01. (Problem.cpp:1446 computes use_benders from mip_settings.benders and data_.size()>200 before the switch on mipSolver, and :1448-1451 returns without consulting mipSolver. benders.cpp:445-447 throws SolverError when DTWC_ENABLE_HIGHS is absent.)
- dtwc/mip/lagrangian_root.cpp:737-749, dtwc/mip/benders.cpp:431-437, dtwc/mip/solution_transaction.cpp:80-86, dtwc/algorithms/fast_pam.cpp:115-128, dtwc/algorithms/fast_clara.cpp:170-180: PLAUSIBLE, not reproduced. Nearest-medoid scans resolve ties to the first slot, so when two medoids are at zero distance (duplicate series, e.g. k=N or k greater than the number of distinct series), the later medoid is labelled into the earlier medoid's cluster. LR-core and Benders then fail publish with SolverError 'medoid ... is not assigned to its own cluster' on a valid optimum. FastPAM and CLARA silently publish an empty cluster. (nearest_medoid uses strict `<`, so the first minimum wins (nearest_medoid.hpp:44-51). LR_core labels every point, medoids included, through it (lagrangian_root.cpp:743-747). validate_result requires labels[medoid]==own cluster (solution_transaction.cpp:80-86). fast_pam compute_nearest_and_second keeps the first d==best. Fix: assign each medoid point to its own slot before the scan (one line per scan). Test: 4 series {a,a,b,c}, k=4, Method::LRCore.)
- dtwc/scores.cpp:104-115: silhouette() on an unclustered Problem prints to stdout and returns N copies of -1, which looks like a valid score vector. davies_bouldin, dunn, inertia and calinski_harabasz throw InvalidInput in the same situation. This is v1 behaviour, but under 2.0's typed-error rule it is a silent wrong answer. 2.0 also dropped v1's thread_local scratch and now heap-allocates `mean_distances` for every point inside the parallel loop. (scores.cpp:110-113: `std::cout << "Please cluster..."; return silhouettes;` (initialised to -1). Compare scores.cpp:180 and :252 (throw InvalidInput). v1.0.0 scores.cpp used `thread_local std::vector<...> mean_distances`; 2.0 scores.cpp:129 constructs a vector per task.)
- dtwc/algorithms/clarans.cpp:115-117,178-181, clarans.hpp:32: CLARANS max_dtw_evals is documented as a 'Hard budget on total DTW computations', but it counts every dist_by_ind lookup, including cached ones, so the budget stops early and bears no relation to DTW work. Moot if CLARANS is deleted (algorithms-mip-03). (`++dtw_evals` follows every prob.dist_by_ind call, including repeat lookups of the cached medoid distances (clarans.cpp:116, 180, and the remaining-medoid loop).)
- dtwc/algorithms/one_batch_pam.cpp:36-50: An explicit batch_size smaller than n_clusters is silently raised to k, with only a stderr line, rather than returning the typed error the no-silent-fallback rule asks for. (warn_batch_size_adjustment prints '...using effective batch_size=' and carries on.)
Verifier on bugs: 1. Method::MIP ignores the selected Solver when N>200: REAL (observed). Problem.cpp:1439 validates mipSolver. :1446 then computes use_benders = benders=="on" || (benders=="auto" && N>200), and :1448-1451 returns MIP_clustering_byBenders without consulting mipSolver. A user who selects Gurobi therefore silently gets HiGHS Benders, and a Gurobi-only build fails a request it could honour. Deleting Benders (-01) fixes it.

2. Tie between medoids at zero distance: REAL by code trace, not executed. nearest_medoid.hpp:44-51 uses strict `<`, so the first tied slot wins. LR_core_clustering labels every point, medoids included, through it (lagrangian_root.cpp:743-747). For two identical series a<b that are both medoids (forced when k=N, or when k exceeds the number of distinct series), point b gets slot(a). validate_result (solution_transaction.cpp:80-86) then throws SolverError 'medoid ... is not assigned to its own cluster' on a valid optimum. fast_pam_swap builds labels from nearest[] (fast_pam.cpp:527-529), where compute_nearest_and_second keeps the first d==best (:117-126), so cluster b is silently published empty; CLARA does the same (fast_clara.cpp:175-179). The HiGHS and Gurobi backends decode labels from x, so they are not affected. Fix: resolve ties toward the point's own slot when the point is a medoid. Decisive test: series {a,a,b,c}, k=4, Method::LRCore (expect SolverError today) and fast_pam (expect an empty cluster).

3. silhouette() on an unclustered Problem: REAL (observed). scores.cpp:111-113 prints to cout and returns N copies of -1. This is identical to v1.0.0 scores.cpp:44-48, so it is v1 behaviour, and changing it to InvalidInput is a behaviour change to a v1 function, which needs a CHANGELOG line. The per-task heap allocation (scores.cpp:129, versus v1's thread_local) is real but minor: O(N) allocations of k pairs against O(N²) work.

4. CLARANS max_dtw_evals counts cached lookups: REAL (observed). ++dtw_evals follows the dist_by_ind calls at clarans.cpp:113-115 and :182-184, and those lookups are lazily cached. The finding is moot under -03.

5. OneBatchPAM raises an explicit batch_size<k to k: REAL (observed). warn_batch_size_adjustment (one_batch_pam.cpp:36-50) writes to stderr and continues. This breaks the typed-error rule for explicit requests only; the auto policy must keep raising to k internally.
Interface notes: How the slice looks to users today:
- Python has a good front door, dtwcpp.cluster(data, k, method=...), with the methods auto|pam|onebatch|clara|kmedoids|mip|lrcore|hierarchical|tadpole (python/dtwcpp/_api.py:374).
- Beside it sits a zoo of direct functions with inconsistent shapes: fast_pam(prob,k,max_iter) and fast_pam_seeded(prob,k,seed,max_iter); fast_clara with positional args; one_batch_pam and one_batch_pam_with_stats; clarans(prob, CLARANSOptions); build_dendrogram and cut_dendrogram (which need a pre-filled matrix); dtw_barycenter and barycenter_kmeans; pdlp_lp_bound and pdlp_gpu_available; and five deprecated score aliases that were never released.
- C++ Method has only 4 values (Kmedoids, MIP, LRCore, TADPole), while the CLI and Python know 9. That makes three dispatch tables (ledger A-01).
- Seeds are `unsigned` in CLARAOptions and CLARANSOptions but `std::uint64_t` in OneBatchPAMOptions, BarycenterOptions and fast_pam_seeded.
- Every algorithm also writes its result into Problem as a side channel (8 copies).
- CLARAOptions mixes algorithm knobs with data-source I/O (parquet_path, ram_limit_bytes, use_float32, force_parquet_streaming). That is the opposite of the owner's 'set device=cpu|gpu|hpc at the start, then the library decides how to load'.
- MIP knobs are stringly-typed (benders="auto|on|off"), and the Solver selection is silently ignored for N>200.

A nicer surface:
(1) One C++ entry point, `core::ClusteringResult cluster(Problem&, int k, Method, std::uint64_t seed = DEFAULT)`, with Method extended to Pam, Clara, OneBatch, Kmedoids, Hierarchical, TADPole, MIP and LRCore. The CLI, Python cluster() and MATLAB dtwc.cluster all call it; it calls Problem::set_result once, and the algorithms stop mutating Problem.
(2) Per-method option structs only where a user really tunes something: CLARA (sample_size, n_samples), OneBatch (batch_size), Hierarchical (linkage), TADPole (dc), Barycenter (method, gamma). All use `std::uint64_t seed` and share max_iter.
(3) Exact routes are MIP (a compact model on the explicitly selected HiGHS or Gurobi) and LRCore (large N, no solver needed). No Benders, no PDLP.
(4) Streaming and out-of-core loading is decided by Problem and Data under device=hpc (from Parquet metadata plus a RAM cap), not by fields on CLARAOptions.
(5) Scores keep the v1 silhouette(Problem&) and add the 2.0 snake_case names only.

## Slice backends — GPU (CUDA, Metal) and MPI backends
Summary: The GPU backends (CUDA 2.9k lines, Metal 2.4k) and MPI (0.34k) are all 2.0-only; none of it shipped in v1.0.0. Production uses only a small part. Problem::fill_distance_matrix (Problem.cpp:1308-1360) calls one function per backend (compute_distance_matrix_cuda / _metal) with band, squared_l2, precision and device_id. Python calls the same two functions. Everything else is reached only from tests and benchmarks:
- 1-vs-N / K-vs-N (about 40% of both files);
- the GPU LB_Keogh pruning and standalone-LB paths;
- KernelOverride and max_length_hint;
- the whole MPI backend.
MPI's design (every rank all-reduces an N×N dense matrix) cannot scale to the target anyway. The fill that does run has avoidable weight: N×N device and host buffers copied element by element into the packed store; a CUDA pair-count guard that refuses N >= 65,537 where chunking would be cheap; four precision vocabularies; checks repeated across three layers; and copied Metal pipeline and wavefront code. The kernel families that earn their place have measurements: CUDA regtile and wavefront (benchmarks/README.md) and Metal regtile, banded_row and wavefront_global (benchmarks/mac_metal_benchmarks.md). The CUDA warp kernel and the double-buffer mode have no isolated measurement. There is one silent wrong answer: Metal returns an all-zero matrix when a scratch allocation fails, and the Python path passes it on to the user. Estimated removal is about 7k lines of library, tests, benchmarks and docs, with no loss to any production caller.

Every rank holds the full N×N double matrix plus local buffers and all-reduces N² mostly-zero entries, so it runs out of memory and bandwidth exactly where MPI would be needed. Every kernel takes the pair count as int and grid.x as int. Problem copies with m.set in an i,j loop (Problem.cpp:1275-1279). Metal normalises N² on the host (metal_dtw.mm:1776-1782). The packed layout tri_index/raw() is shared by DenseDistanceMatrix and MmapDistanceMatrix (mmap_distance_matrix.hpp:41,147,696), so the memcpy target works for both variant arms. Caveats: (1) This is a rework (+90 lines) that changes a ledger G-11 DO-NOT-BREAK item ('direct N×N write-back'). The packed index makes writes non-coalesced, so it needs a registered before/after benchmark on the RTX 4000 Ada, not 'perf_risk low' on assertion. (2) Every Metal MSL kernel's output write changes, and Metal is BLOCKED-ENV locally. (3) CUDA chunking needs an int64 pair_offset argument on every pairwise kernel. (4) The 2^31 narrowing bug that chunk_dispatch pins was real. Inlining is fine only as an explicitly typed `const std::int64_t pair_offset = static_cast<std::int64_t>(off);`. (5) dtwc/test_api.hpp gpu() checks res.matrix.size()==N*N and must be updated. Do it after -01/-02 so dead kernels are not rewritten.

- **backends-M3** benchmarks/bench_cuda_dtw.cpp:247-285 (BM_cuda_prunedDistanceMatrix), benchmarks/bench_metal_dtw.cpp:170-228 (BM_metal_lb_keogh_permissive/strict) (-95): Benchmark callers of GPU LB pruning that backends-02 does not list. They would not compile once use_lb_keogh and lb_threshold are removed. → Delete them with backends-02.
Keep (looks like bloat, earns its place):
- dtwc::detail::decode_pair as the single host/device/MSL pair decoder (kDecodePairMSL prepended to the Metal source): design.md §9 invariant; the retired per-backend copies were wrong (FP32 decode, int32 index).
- thread_local DTWLaunchWorkspace with growing device buffers and CachedHostBuffer (pinned above 256 KiB, pageable if pinned allocation fails): Performance (no per-call cudaMalloc, overlapping async copies). The pageable fallback changes speed only, never results, so it is not the forbidden silent fallback.
- CUDA regtile<4>/<8> and the wavefront kernel (preload ≤512, 3-buffer, persistent grid): Measured 60-75 Gcells/s across L (benchmarks/README.md:20-29). The >2048 three-buffer cap is a correctness fix (Task 0.1).
- Metal dtw_banded_row, dtw_wavefront_global and regtile_w4/w8: banded_row measured 1.38× over wavefront at 75×10000, band=100 (mac_metal_benchmarks.md:87-91). wavefront_global is required above the 32 KB threadgroup cap (L>2730). regtile has an M2 Max measurement (changelog).
- Metal 5e9-cell chunking per command buffer and waits between chunks when scratch is shared: Correctness on macOS: the GPU watchdog kills command buffers longer than about 2 s, and a shared scratch buffer would be overwritten by the next chunk.
- require_shared_mem_fits (CUDA): A reachable hardware limit (long series or small GPUs). It costs one check per launch and turns a bare 'invalid argument' into an actionable DeviceError.
- One device-availability check at the backend entry throwing DeviceError: Without it a missing device used to produce a valid-looking zero matrix (A16). Keep exactly one layer (see backends-07).
- validate_fill_request / validate_gpu_request GPU rejections (non-Standard variant, missing strategy, ndim>1, Float32 storage, mmap/view data, Metal FP64, Metal index≠0): Builder-side guards required by the no-silent-fallback rule. Without them the GPU would silently compute Standard univariate DTW (ledger G-04).
- cuda_memory.cuh RAII types (CudaPtr, PinnedPtr, CudaStream, CudaEvent): The repo forbids naked new/delete in core. These are the smallest correct owners, not an extra abstraction layer.
- dtwc::test::gpu() self-probe (dtwc/test_api.hpp) behind dtwcpp.test.gpu / MATLAB test_gpu: Lets a user on a new HPC node check that the GPU build computes the same as the CPU oracle. Worth keeping, but route it through the single gpu fill entry once backends-05/12 land.
- Per-device cache of device attributes (in some form): cudaGetDeviceProperties is slow and was called per launch. backends-08 keeps the cache but moves it into the existing thread_local workspace.
Silent bugs reported:
- dtwc/metal/metal_dtw.mm:1436-1453,1491-1504; python/src/_dtwcpp_core.cpp:102-110,1737-1756; python/dtwcpp/__init__.py:258-261: On a Metal scratch allocation failure, compute_distance_matrix_metal returns an all-zero N×N matrix (pairs_computed=0) and the verbose message claims a CPU fallback. Problem catches it (Problem.cpp:1265), but the Python path dtwcpp.compute_distance_matrix(..., device='gpu') on macOS returns the zeros to the user, because checked_square_matrix only checks the size. (Read the code: result.matrix.resize(N*N, 0.0); pairs_computed = 0; return result. The binding checks only matrix.size() == n*n. Ledger G-01 is still open.)
- dtwc/metal/metal_dtw.mm:~186-194 (dtw_wavefront final read cur[La-1]; same pattern in wavefront_global/kvn); python/src/_dtwcpp_core.cpp:115-120: An empty series in the Python direct GPU path makes the Metal kernel read cur[-1] (out of bounds in threadgroup memory), which gives a garbage distance. require_finite_series does not reject empty series; only Problem does (Problem.hpp:299-310). CUDA returns the max sentinel for the same input instead (cuda_dtw.cu:227-238), so the backends disagree. ([inferred from code, not run; Metal is BLOCKED-ENV locally] La=0 ⇒ `float result = cur[La - 1]`. Ledger G-02 is still open. Fix: reject empty series at the single GPU entry (backends-12), the same check as Problem::reject_empty_series.)
- dtwc/cuda/gpu_config.cuh:94-101: The FP64-speed table classifies every compute capability with minor==0 at or above 7 as full-rate. Consumer Blackwell (sm_120, RTX 50-series) therefore counts as full-rate, and Auto precision picks FP64 there, which is much slower. This is a performance issue, not a wrong answer. (Read the table: `if (prop.major >= 7) cfg.fp64_rate = (prop.minor == 0) ? Full : Slow;`. The fix is in backends-08 (cudaDevAttrSingleToDoublePrecisionPerfRatio).)
- dtwc/mpi/mpi_distance_matrix.cpp:124-139,40-47: MPI truncates the per-rank pair count to int in the OpenMP loop. On one rank with N≈100k (5e9 pairs) it computes only part of the matrix, and the rest stays 0. It also calls MPI_Init lazily from the library and never calls MPI_Finalize. This is reachable only from the benchmark and tests; it becomes moot if backends-03 deletes MPI. (Read `for (int idx = 0; idx < static_cast<int>(local_count); ++idx)`. Ledger G-03 is still open.)
- dtwc/metal/metal_dtw.mm:1517-1524,1566-1570,1360-1383: Metal silently turns off requested LB_Keogh pruning when regtile or banded_row is selected, or when a buffer allocation fails, and silently ignores unsupported KernelOverride values. Distances stay correct, but the pruned-to-NaN contract the caller asked for does not hold, against the no-silent-fallback rule. This becomes moot if backends-02 and -04 delete these features. (Read the cited branches; the only signal is a std::cerr line under verbose.)
Verifier on bugs: 1) Metal returns an all-zero matrix on a scratch allocation failure: REAL. metal_dtw.mm:1443-1452 and 1495-1503 print 'falling back to CPU' and then do result.matrix.resize(N*N, 0.0); pairs_computed = 0; return. On the Python path, __init__.py:258-261 calls _compute_distance_matrix_metal, and _dtwcpp_core.cpp:1752 runs checked_square_matrix, which checks only size() == n*n (:102-110). The zeros therefore reach the user. Problem catches it at Problem.cpp:1264.
2) Metal reads cur[-1] on an empty series: PLAUSIBLY REAL, inferred from code and not run (Metal is BLOCKED-ENV). The dtw_wavefront path computes K = La+Lb-1 and then reads `float result = cur[La - 1]` (metal_dtw.mm:186); there is no La==0 guard in the four wavefront kernels (i_hi = min(La-1,k) at :135/251/502/598). CUDA guards M==0 and writes INF (cuda_dtw.cu:227-238). require_finite_series does not reject empty input; Problem does (Problem.hpp:302). Related inconsistency: when ALL series are empty, CUDA returns early from max_L==0 with a zero matrix (cuda_dtw.cu:1470), but it returns INF when only some are empty.
3) Blackwell sm_120 counted as full-rate FP64: REAL, performance only. gpu_config.cuh:94-95 applies `major >= 7 → minor == 0 ? Full : Slow`, so CC 12.0 → Full and Auto picks FP64.
4) MPI int truncation: REAL, but reachable only from the benchmark and tests. mpi_distance_matrix.cpp:124 casts with `static_cast<int>(local_count)`. With 1 rank and N=1e5, local_count ≈ 5e9 wraps to 705,032,704, so about 14% of pairs are computed and the rest stay 0 through the SUM Allreduce. The library calls MPI_Init lazily (:36-43) and never finalizes; the callers do. Moot if backends-03 lands.
5) Metal silently disables LB pruning and ignores unsupported overrides: REAL at runtime. The pinned lines include `lb_active = false;` (check_docs_contract.py:877-880). It is documented as a 'degrade' in gpu-backends.md:265-270, but it still breaks the no-silent-fallback rule. Moot after -02/-04.
MISSED SILENT BUG: dtwcpp.compute_distance_matrix(series, device='gpu:1') on a Metal build silently computes on the default GPU. _resolve_device returns ('metal', 1) (__init__.py:145-146), but __init__.py:259 passes no device_id and the compute_distance_matrix_metal binding has no device_id parameter (_dtwcpp_core.cpp:1737-1740). Problem instead raises DeviceError for a Metal index other than 0 (Problem.cpp:189-193; contract §6.4, lines 901-902). Fix it in backends-12's single GPU entry.
Interface notes: How the slice looks to users today:
- C++: two parallel low-level namespaces (dtwc::cuda, dtwc::metal), each with its own options, result and precision types, 1-vs-N/K-vs-N/LB extras and a KernelOverride knob. The umbrella dtwc.hpp includes them (and MPI) behind #ifdefs. Problem selects the GPU two ways: set_device(Device::GPU, i) and distance_strategy = CUDA/Metal. Its settings struct is called CUDASettings even though Metal reads it, and precision is a bare int 0/1/2.
- Python: cuda_available, cuda_device_info, CUDA_AVAILABLE, metal_available, metal_device_info, METAL_AVAILABLE, compute_lb_keogh_cuda and MPI_AVAILABLE are all public. When a backend is not built, stubs throw std::runtime_error.
- MATLAB: set_cuda_settings(device_id, precision) and 'cuda'/'metal' strategy strings.
- CLI: device=gpu, which goes to PAM on the GPU; CLARA is refused.
- MPI: nothing at all.

A nicer surface, matching the owner's 'device=cpu|gpu|hpc, then the library decides':
1. Users say only device='gpu[:N]' and optionally gpu_precision=auto|fp32|fp64 (one GpuPrecision enum across C++, Python, MATLAB and CLI).
2. Inside, one always-compiled fill translation unit holds all backend #ifdefs. It exposes fill_gpu(series, FillOptions, PairRange, packed_out), plus gpu_available() and gpu_info(). It picks CUDA, else Metal, else throws DeviceError. Problem, the Python compute_distance_matrix and the self-test all call it.
3. DistanceMatrixStrategy gains GPU; CUDA and Metal become deprecated spellings of it.
4. device='hpc' means SLURM submission (exists) plus a sharded PairRange fill (`--shard i/n` / `--merge`) that writes packed slices in the checkpoint format. MPI is not needed.
5. The low-level dtwc::cuda / dtwc::metal namespaces become implementation detail (not in dtwc.hpp). Each keeps one entry, compute_distance_matrix_*, used by benchmarks. kernel_used stays on the result for benchmark reporting.

Separately, GPU input is Float64 only (Float32 storage is rejected) even though consumer GPUs compute in FP32. Accepting Float32 storage for FP32 kernels would halve host-to-device traffic. That is a later additive change, not a cut.

## Slice bindings — Python package and MATLAB package / MEX
Summary: Python package (nanobind core 1891 lines + ~4.2k lines of pure Python) and MATLAB package (MEX 1827 + ~2.4k lines of .m). Nothing here was in v1.0.0 except a thin pybind `Problem`/`DataLoader` wrapper with camelCase names, and the 2.0 binding does not keep those names. Most of the bloat is re-implementation. Python's Tier-1 (`_api.py`) re-does C++ `dtwc::cluster`/`run(Config)`: method aliases, `auto` resolution, dispatch, score and byte-identical save. MATLAB already makes that one call (`tier1_cluster`), so Python is the outlier. The `device="hpc"` path carries 20 Config fields through four hand-written layers (Python kwargs, bash positional args, sbatch env exports, CLI flags), and validates them three times with integer-range guards. `dtwc_cl` already reads the same fields from a `--config` file that `to_config_text()` writes. Other bloat: two sklearn estimators; deprecation aliases for names that were never released (Python and MATLAB); per-variant distance and distance-matrix routes, while the C++ `core::dtw_runtime` (written for the bindings) has no binding caller and `Problem::set_metric` is not bound; telemetry-specific helper modules (`preprocess`/`diagnose`/`features`); a Python second writer of the `.dtws` format; dead MEX commands and a 537-line script test that CI never runs. Core plumbing is sound and should stay: capsule ownership, GIL release, error taxonomy, MEX handle map and guards. Estimated net cut is about 5–6k lines, library plus tests.

MATLAB (DTWClustering.m:153-186) recomputes on every L1 path, GPU included. The action (one Problem, n_init seeded runs) is right. The hpc branch in fit (_clustering.py:353-370) must move to the bindings-02 transport.

Name tables exist in dtwc/base/names.hpp:49 (parse_name) and in the enum headers. There are 32 raw static_cast<int>(get_scalar(...)) calls. Two parts of the action are wrong. (1) 'Drop the INT_MIN special case in to_0based' (dtwc_mex.cpp:290-296) would bring back signed-overflow UB: INT_MIN passes exact_int_from_double, and INT_MIN - 1 overflows. Replace the special case with a `value >= 1` index check (a 1-based index must be positive), which covers it; do not drop it. (2) The per-field validators on DTWVariantParams and CUDASettings are not simply redundant. The getters return const& with nanobind's reference_internal policy, so a nested write such as prob.variant_params.wdtw_g = x changes Problem state in place without going through set_variant. test_problem_semantic_transactions.py:140-173 and test_problem.py:133 pin the reconciliation of nested writes at the compute boundary.   What: MATLAB Result.plot obtains the distance matrix by calling Result_save into a temp directory (writing all four CSVs plus silhouettes) and reading the matrix CSV back with readmatrix, while C++ Result::distance_matrix() exists.
  Evidence: Result.m distance_matrix(): `dtwc_mex('Result_save', obj.Handle, scratch); obj.Dmat = readmatrix(...)`. dtwc/api.hpp:93 Result::distance_matrix().

  Verifier: Result.m:107-121 calls Result_save into a tempname directory and reads the matrix CSV back with readmatrix. This path fails if the matrix holds inf (the save preflight refuses it), and it writes silhouettes as a side effect. C++ Result::distance_matrix() exists (api.hpp:93). The MEX has only Result_score, Result_save and Result_delete (dispatch :1750-1752). A MEX Result_distance_matrix with a row-major to column-major copy is about 10 lines.
Keep (looks like bloat, earns its place):
- adopt_as_ndarray (unique_ptr -> nb::capsule ownership transfer) in _dtwcpp_core.cpp: Zero-copy hand-off of owned buffers to numpy with no leak window; every matrix/array return should use it.
- nb::gil_scoped_release around every C++ compute/IO call: Lets other Python threads run during N^2 fills; needed for performance and responsiveness.
- Error taxonomy: DtwcError + InvalidInput/UndefinedScore/SolverError/DeviceError/IOError with dual built-in bases, one exception translator (Python), and the ordered catch chain mapping to dtwc:* ids (MEX): The typed-error, no-silent-fallback contract depends on it, and the MEX catch order is load-bearing (InvalidInput derives from runtime_error).
- data_from_arrow_c_array (nanoarrow PyCapsule __arrow_c_array__/__arrow_c_stream__ ingest): Zero-copy Parquet/polars/DuckDB input without a pyarrow dependency, and the right replacement for io.py's Parquet wrappers.
- _read_data binding over the C++ DataLoader: The single reader shared by C++, CLI, Python and (after bindings-09) MATLAB.
- MEX HandleManager + mexAtExit drain + mexLock, and the longjmp-safe error mapping in mexFunction: Required for correct lifetime of C++ objects behind MATLAB handles; RAII objects must be destroyed before mexErrMsgIdAndTxt longjmps.
- MEX require_real_double / require_label_vector / exact_int_from_double: Real crash and UB fixes: mxGetDoubles returns NULL for non-double input, and casting a NaN double to int is UB. These are type-conversion checks, not count guards.
- macOS __kmpc_dispatch_deinit shim and libomp install_name rewrite (dtwc_mex.cpp:22-29, bindings/matlab/CMakeLists.txt): Without them the MEX does not load on MATLAB R2026a on macOS.
- cmd_tier1_cluster / Result_score / Result_save in the MEX: The one-route Tier-1 design Python should copy (bindings-01).
- dtwcpp.test.parallelisation()/gpu() and MATLAB +test/*, backed by dtwc::test::*: Real probes (engaged thread ids, GPU kernel vs CPU oracle), not flag reads; the wheel release gate uses them.
- python/dtwcpp/_wheel_smoke.py inside the package: cibuildwheel runs it against the installed wheel (python-wheels.yml:94); it must be importable from the wheel.
- _hpc.write_series_tsv with repr(), parse_labels_csv by name, the _SAFE_JOB_NAME/_SAFE_REMOTE_PATH regexes, leading-'-' refusal, shell_join/remote_argv quoting, and the mktemp allocator check: Full-precision data transfer and the shell/SSH injection boundary are genuine. Only the Config-field validation is redundant (bindings-02).
- The global dtwcpp.device() / dtwc.device() pair over dtwc::device(): It is the owner's preferred 'device at the start' entry point; keep it, and delete the parallel Env/Device APIs (bindings-11).
Silent bugs reported:
- bindings/matlab/dtwc_mex.cpp:644,664,670,676,777-778,1142-1239,1289-1354,1592-1598; bindings/matlab/+dtwc/fast_pam.m: 32 raw `static_cast<int>(get_scalar(...))` conversions silently truncate fractional MATLAB arguments, and NaN/Inf/out-of-range values are undefined behaviour. prob.set_n_clusters(2.5) runs k=2; dtwc.fast_pam(prob, 2.7) runs k=2 (its inputParser checks only v > 0); dist_by_ind(1.5, 2) reads index 0; band=NaN is UB. (get_exact_int (dtwc_mex.cpp:282) exists and is used only at a few sites; .claude/reports/2026-09-21-multiagent-sweep.md:388 already proposed the one-line swaps, and they are not applied (line 644 is still a raw cast).)
- bindings/matlab/+dtwc/DTWClustering.m (fit); bindings/matlab/dtwc_mex.cpp:1562-1585: DTWClustering('Metric','squared_euclidean','Device','gpu') computes the full matrix on the CPU through core::compute_distance_matrix_pruned and injects it into a GPU Problem, so the GPU never runs. This is a silent CPU fallback, against the no-silent-fallback rule. fit() also temporarily changes the process-wide device (restored only by onCleanup). (DTWClustering.m: `if ~strcmp(metric,'l1') precomputed = dtwc_mex('DTWClustering_compute_distance_matrix', ...)`, then prob.set_device(activeDevice) and prob.set_distance_matrix(precomputed). cmd_DTWClustering_compute_distance_matrix calls the CPU-only pruned builder.)
- python/dtwcpp/_clustering.py:477-483: DTWClustering.score(X) calls self.fit(X) and so silently refits: it overwrites labels_/medoid_indices_/cluster_centers_ and pays a full O(N^2) fit. Scoring a held-out set replaces the fitted model. sklearn's score must not mutate the estimator (DTWCKMedoids.score does not). (`def score(self, X, y=None): self.fit(X, y); return -self.inertia_`)
- python/dtwcpp/_clustering.py (fit restart loop); bindings/matlab/+dtwc/DTWClustering.m (for rep = 1:obj.NInit): n_init restarts rebuild a Problem per restart, so the full N^2 DTW matrix is recomputed n_init times. The answer is correct but costs n_init times as much; the matrix is seed-independent. (_clustering.py `prob = self._build_problem(series, ...)` inside `for restart in range(restart_count)`; fast_pam.cpp:580 prob.fill_distance_matrix() on each fresh Problem.)
- python/src/_dtwcpp_core.cpp (Problem binding); .claude/design.md:41; .claude/DECISIONS.md:153: A contract breach, not silent-wrong: design.md marks 'the v1.0.0 pybind names such as refreshDistanceMatrix, cluster_size' FROZEN and DECISIONS says they keep shims, but none is bound. The 2.0 `cluster_size` is a property, so v1.0.0 code `p.cluster_size()` raises TypeError and `p.refreshDistanceMatrix()` raises AttributeError. (git show v1.0.0:python/py_main.cpp binds cluster_size as a method plus ~25 camelCase names; git grep refreshDistanceMatrix -- python: 0 hits.)
- python/dtwcpp/_hpc.py (_validate_remote_configuration); python/dtwcpp/_slurm/slurm_remote.sh:584-587: device='hpc' refuses metric='squared_euclidean' on the CPU ('unsupported by the remote CPU CLI') although the CLI now runs CPU squared L2 (IF-2). This is a wrong refusal of a supported configuration: it fails loudly, it is not silent. (PLAN.md:483 'CPU squared L2 runs'; _hpc.py `if not is_cuda and metric != "l1": raise ValueError(...)`.)
Verifier on bugs: 1) MEX raw casts: REAL. dtwc_mex.cpp has 32 `static_cast<int>(get_scalar(...))` calls. Examples: :644 set_band, :676 set_n_clusters, :777-778 dist_by_ind, :1289 fast_pam k. Problem.m:157-160 and :151-155 pass double(k), double(i) and double(j) through unchecked, and fast_pam.m:45 checks only v > 0. So a value of 2.5 truncates to 2, and NaN, Inf or out-of-range values are UB. get_exact_int (:282) exists but these sites do not use it.
2) MATLAB DTWClustering squared_euclidean + gpu runs on the CPU: REAL. DTWClustering.m:139-142 builds the matrix through cmd_DTWClustering_compute_distance_matrix, which calls the CPU-only core::compute_distance_matrix_pruned (dtwc_mex.cpp:1570). It then injects that matrix into a GPU Problem (:158-180). The CUDA kernels do support squared L2 (cuda_dtw.cu:125/292 use_squared_l2), so this is a silent CPU fallback. The process-wide device change is restored by onCleanup (:125-126).
3) DTWClustering.score refits: REAL. _clustering.py:477-483 is `self.fit(X, y); return -self.inertia_`, which overwrites the fitted state.
4) n_init recomputes the matrix: PARTLY REAL (a performance issue; the result is correct). Python recomputes only on the default CPU-L1 path; GPU and non-L1 fits precompute once and inject (_clustering.py:376-390). fast_pam_seeded refills each fresh Problem (fast_pam.cpp:580). MATLAB recomputes on every L1 path, GPU included (DTWClustering.m:153-186).
5) v1 shim contract breach: REAL. design.md:41 marks the v1.0.0 pybind names (refreshDistanceMatrix, cluster_size) FROZEN, and DECISIONS.md:153 says they keep shims. v1.0.0 py_main.cpp:24-49 binds them as methods; none is bound today. The v1.0.0 Python was never on PyPI (design.md PRE-TAG row), which bears on the owner's decision.
6) hpc refuses CPU squared_euclidean: REAL, and loud rather than silent. _hpc.py:167-169 and slurm_remote.sh:584-587 refuse it, but run.cpp:458 calls prob.set_metric(config.metric), and PLAN.md:483 records 'CPU squared L2 runs'.
Interface notes: How the slice looks to a user today. There are three ways to cluster in Python: dtwcpp.cluster, DTWClustering and DTWCKMedoids, plus Tier-2 Problem. They validate differently, and device and hpc work differently in each. dtwcpp.cluster accepts only k/method/band/device/max_iter, while the CLI accepts ~30 options. The device is set in three ways: dtwcpp.device(), Env/env(), and Problem(device=). Deprecated aliases for names that were never released still warn, while the v1.0.0 names the design promises are missing. MATLAB mixes PascalCase and snake_case name-value keys, supports only the L1 metric in dtwc.distance.*, cannot select lrcore/tadpole on a Problem, and ships a script test on the user path. Distance and matrix functions differ per language: MATLAB has no metric or device on compute_distance_matrix, and neither language has MSM/TWE pairwise.

A nicer surface, where one C++ Config is the single vocabulary shared by the CLI, a TOML/YAML file, Python kwargs and MATLAB name-value:

Python:
```
import dtwcpp as dtwc
dtwc.device("gpu")                      # cpu | gpu | gpu:N | hpc, once
res = dtwc.cluster("data.csv", k=5, method="auto", band=10, metric="l1", variant="standard")  # kwargs == dtwc_cl flags == Config fields -> run(Config)
res.labels; res.medoids; res.cost; res.score("silhouette"); res.save("out/"); res.plot()
D = dtwc.distance_matrix(X, band=10, metric="l1")          # Problem fill, device-aware
d = dtwc.distance.dtw(x, y, variant="msm", msm_c=0.5)       # one dtw_runtime binding
est = dtwc.DTWClustering(n_clusters=5, n_init=3, metric="precomputed" | "l1" | ...)  # the one estimator
prob = dtwc.Problem(device="gpu")                              # Tier-2 for experts
```
On hpc, the same cluster() call writes the Config with to_config_text and ships it (bindings-02).

MATLAB: `dtwc.device('gpu'); res = dtwc.cluster('data.csv', 5, 'band', 10, 'metric', 'l1'); res.score('silhouette'); D = dtwc.distance_matrix(X, 'band', 10); d = dtwc.distance.dtw(x, y, 'variant', 'msm');`. The keys are the same snake_case names; tier1_cluster widens to take the Config name-value list.

C++ and CLI are already there (dtwc::cluster / run(Config) / Result, dtwc_cl --config). The bindings should become thin: data conversion, the GIL, the error map and handles. They should not keep their own validation, method routing, enum parsing or file formats. Owner decision needed: whether to keep the v1.0.0 camelCase Python Problem shims (about 25 one-line forwards) or record the break.

## Slice tests-build — Tests, gate scripts, CMake, CI, benchmarks, examples
Summary: This slice has about 95k tracked lines: tests about 76k (tests/unit 57.4k, of which adversarial/ is 9.6k; python 8.9k; matlab 3.0k; integration 3.8k), scripts 6.7k, benchmarks 8.2k (about 3.2k of that is committed result JSON), cmake 1.9k, CI 0.95k and examples 1.4k. The core harness is sound and worth keeping: dtwc_add_test passes a test only if at least one assertion ran with no skip, the real-binary CLI scripts cover the CLI, a cross-language conformance reference exists, the exhaustive LB derivation tests are strong, and GPU tests are checked against a host oracle.

The bloat has five sources:
(1) Meta-gates that pin text rather than behaviour: a supply-chain parser plus its own tests (about 2.1k lines), a docs gate that pins doc sentences to code (1.85k), and tests that read source files.
(2) The same contract tested in 3–9 files, with adversarial/ duplicating the subject files. There are 9 lower-bound files, 5 enum-validation files that check every layer, and 3 wave-named integration files.
(3) Dead or unregistered one-off tooling: a mutation harness, MATLAB oracles nobody calls, Python scripts that run in neither CTest nor CI, and one-off benchmarks and result dumps.
(4) Hard-coded marker and counter theatre.
(5) Tests that pin overflow guards the owner now rules out.

Estimated removal is about 24k lines net (tests 76k to about 50k, scripts 6.7k to about 3.5k, benchmarks 8.2k to about 5.5k) with no loss of an independent-oracle contract. One silent fallback exists in the build: CUDA=ON on macOS only warns and turns itself off.

  What: These tests pin integer-overflow guards and historical int32 bugs.   Verifier: (a) The FastCLARA case (unit_test_fast_clara.cpp:364-398) has two halves. One is int arithmetic on a user-supplied k ('10*k+100 overflows a 32-bit int'), which is signed-overflow UB from input. The other is 'N exceeds the int-indexed clustering result limit', which guards int-typed labels and medoids. It becomes dead only once the library moves the index type to 64-bit per the owner's rule. Deleting the guard first turns a typed error into truncation for Parquet streams with N > INT_MAX. (b) old_metal_fp32_decode and the two historical cases (:219-276) can go. (c) CUDA A15 guards a real third-party limit, reachable at N=65537, which is inside the 100M-series target. It is a typed error (no silent fallback). Chunking is a new library feature, and the test changes only when that lands. (d) Shrinking the MPI test is fine: it is 6 cases in 140 lines.

The library uses llfio only for mapped_file create/open/extend, a barrier (flush) and a session lease (file lock).

Deleting the patch is therefore the likely end state'. `git grep llfio:: -- dtwc`: 12 uses in mmap_data_store.hpp and 23 in mmap_distance_matrix.hpp, all mapped_file_handle, barrier and io_request.

The facts are right: Dependencies.cmake:236-246 says the quickcpplib patch 'may now be spent'. pyproject.toml:73 and every artefact workflow set LLFIO=OFF, so wheel users get no mmap. llfio:: has 35 uses in 2 headers. But mio (mandreyel/mio) is less maintained than llfio, and the owner's standing preference is maintained libraries over custom platform code. A hand-rolled flock/LockFileEx lease plus a 120-line mmap is custom platform code. The durability (barrier) semantics also need an equivalent.

Keep (looks like bloat, earns its place):
- cmake/DtwcTest.cmake dtwc_add_test / dtwc_add_script_test (185 lines): This harness is what lets CTest distinguish 'ran' from 'skipped' (a pass needs >=1 assertion in >=1 case, no skip unless MAY_SKIP; REQUIRES registers nothing when a capability is absent). It replaces 13 hand-written skip blocks. Only the MARKER-on-Catch2 usage should go (finding 12).
- Real-binary CLI integration scripts: test_cli_device_matrix, test_cli_config_formats, test_cli_loud_failures, test_cli_resume_state (+ f17_checkpoint_writer), test_distance_matrix_csv_contract (+ f14 writer), test_fast_clara_parquet_parity / assignment_contract (+ f13 writer), test_cli_rejects_unknown_option: These are the only proof that the shipped dtwc_cl binary is reachable and fails loudly (ledger T-21). Each takes under 10 s. The duplicated require_occurrences/require_no_skip/run_cli helpers (2 copies each) could go into tests/integration/common.cmake, but that is small.
- tests/conformance (cpp_conformance.cpp + conformance_reference.txt + data + test_conformance.py/.m): This is the only independent cross-language oracle: C++, Python and MATLAB are all checked against one tracked, read-only reference, and regeneration is explicit and fails on purpose (T-15 fixed, cpp_conformance.cpp:212-237). Keep it and wire the Python half into CI (silent bug 2).
- Exhaustive LB derivation tests (test_lb_keogh_derivation.cpp, test_lb_enhanced_webb_derivation.cpp): They enumerate every warping path for small n, so they are the strongest oracle in the suite for LB admissibility. An inadmissible bound prunes wrongly, which is a silently wrong answer. Only the counters and markers are fluff.
- GPU correctness tests + tests/unit/gpu_fixed_band_oracle.hpp; test_decode_pair brute-force + MSL-parity cases: GPU tests are checked against a host full-matrix DP; they are MAY_SKIP and print a reason. decode_pair is the single host+device pair decoder (design §9), and the MSL string parity case is the only thing tying the Metal shader to the host decoder.
- tests/support/deterministic_series.hpp: The brief's hypothesis that no test uses it is false. `git grep` shows it is included by 7 test files (CUDA/Metal correctness and LB, mpi, lower_bounds, deterministic_series) and 5 benchmarks, and it is the portable, toolchain-independent generator (F15). Keep its three hash pins, but drop the source-audit case (finding 10).
- test_deprecated_shims_warn (compile probe, tests/CMakeLists.txt:14-38 + integration/deprecated_shims_probe.cc + .cmake): The C++ 1.x names (set_numberOfClusters, distByInd, fillDistanceMatrix, ...) shipped in v1.0.0, and this ~110-line probe is the only proof they still compile and warn on each compiler family.
- MPI Allreduce chunking (mpi/allreduce_chunking.hpp) and its test (shrunk): N*N exceeds INT_MAX at N≈46k, which a real run can reach, and MPI counts are int. This is exactly the owner's 'chunk where we own the loop' case, not a panic guard.
- unit_test_mmap_distance_matrix 'open rejects N that overflows packed size' (:1046): This validates an untrusted on-disk header, which is input validation at the boundary, not an internal count guard.
- scripts/machine_facts.py, scripts/run_bench.sh, scripts/smoke_release_archive.py, scripts/generate_docs.py, scripts/check_site_links.py, scripts/build_libomp_macos.sh, scripts/repo_map.py: Benchmark provenance (the dtwc-run-benchmarks skill), the release-archive smoke test in release-artifacts.yml, generated docs pages with a --check mode, a site link check, the libomp build for portable wheels, and the layer report the runbook uses. Each has a live caller.
- CI: 10 workflows, 953 lines: They are small and each covers a distinct leg: 3 OS unit legs, the Arrow leg, the CUDA configure+build in an nvcc container, the MPI build+run, MATLAB MEX, Python tests, wheels, release archives and docs+coverage. The only wart is the macOS-CUDA 'graceful rejection' job (silent bug 1).
- tests/CMakeLists.txt Windows Arrow runtime PATH block (:586-624): Without it, Arrow tests on Windows cannot find arrow.dll or pyarrow.libs, and they fail for a reason unrelated to the code.
- tests/python/test_hpc.py (1469): device='hpc' is a headline user feature (CHARTER). The file drives the real _hpc.py and packaged slurm_remote.sh against stubbed ssh/scp/sbatch and checks the argv they receive. It is large but has no sibling duplicate. Trim it only when _hpc.py shrinks.
- The dtwc_* to DTWC_* maintainer-option rename shim (ProjectOptions.cmake:17-41): The lowercase dtwc_ENABLE_* options did ship in v1.0.0 (`git show v1.0.0:cmake/ProjectOptions.cmake`). The shim is one 20-line macro with a one-release sunset, so it can stay until 2.1.
Silent bugs reported:
- CMakeLists.txt:79-88; .github/workflows/cuda-mpi-detect.yml (macos-cuda-graceful-rejection job): On macOS, -DDTWC_ENABLE_CUDA=ON prints a WARNING and sets DTWC_ENABLE_CUDA OFF, and configure succeeds. A user who asked for CUDA gets a CPU build, which violates the non-negotiable 'no silent fallback; a request that cannot be honoured is a typed error'. The CI job pins this behaviour by grepping for 'not supported on macOS' after a successful configure. The comment at CMakeLists.txt:52-53 likewise says DTWC_ENABLE_METAL is 'silently ignored on non-Apple platforms', so an explicit -DDTWC_ENABLE_METAL=ON on Linux cannot be told apart from the default. (CMakeLists.txt:81-88 opened: `if(APPLE) message(WARNING "CUDA is not supported on macOS ... Disabling CUDA.") set(DTWC_ENABLE_CUDA OFF)`. cuda-mpi-detect.yml: `cmake ... -DDTWC_ENABLE_CUDA=ON ... | tee cmake_output.txt; grep -q "not supported on macOS"`. Fix: message(FATAL_ERROR) for CUDA on Apple, and for METAL=ON on non-Apple, make the default `$<APPLE>` via `if(APPLE)` so an explicit ON elsewhere is an error. Change the CI job to expect a non-zero configure.)
- tests/conformance/test_conformance.py; .github/workflows/python-tests.yml:36; pyproject.toml (no [tool.pytest] testpaths): The Python leg of the cross-language conformance gate never runs. CI runs `pytest tests/python/` only, the file lives in tests/conformance/, and no testpaths setting collects it. So 'Python matches the C++ reference' is asserted nowhere, and the C++ and MATLAB legs can go green while Python drifts. The same holds for tests/integration/test_cross_language.py. (`git grep -l test_conformance.py -- .github tests/CMakeLists.txt pyproject.toml tests/python` returns 0 hits. python-tests.yml:36 is `uv run pytest tests/python/ -v`. The MATLAB twin does run (matlab-mex.yml references test_conformance.m). Fix: move the file to tests/python/test_conformance.py, resolving the reference via Path(__file__).parents[1]/'conformance', or add `testpaths = ["tests/python", "tests/conformance"]`.)
- tests/unit/adversarial/test_scores_adversarial.cpp:228; tests/unit/adversarial/test_arow_adversarial.cpp:619; tests/unit/adversarial/test_lb_enhanced_webb.cpp:513: These cases pass without checking anything. The assertions sit inside `if(!isnan(...))`, the case has no assertion at all, or it prints 'FALSIFIED' and exits 0. If the score really returned NaN, the test would still be green. (Ledger T-17 (2026-09-07-diff-ledger.md:173) lists these line numbers. The GT-1 harness requires >=1 assertion per binary, not per case, so a vacuous case inside a file with other assertions still passes. Fixed as part of finding 4 (delete or give each case an unconditional oracle).)
Verifier on bugs: Bug 1 (CUDA on macOS warns and disables): REAL as a policy violation, and wider than reported. CMakeLists.txt:81-88 does warn-and-disable on Apple. The same pattern appears in: CUDA requested but nvcc not found on Linux/Windows (CMakeLists.txt:215-218); MPI requested but not found (Dependencies.cmake:524-528); llfio requested but not found, which 'falls back to in-memory store' (Dependencies.cmake:383); Arrow CPM failure or Windows+Clang (Dependencies.cmake:429, :490); and METAL=ON silently turned off on non-Apple (CMakeLists.txt:229-232). A consequence: the CI job linux-cuda-configure (cuda-mpi-detect.yml:10-28) would still go green if CUDA detection failed, because configure only warns and the build then compiles without CUDA. It does not prove CUDA was built. It is not a silent wrong answer at runtime: a GPU request in a CPU-only build is refused (python/src/_dtwcpp_core.cpp:1709 throws, and test_cli_device_matrix expects a refusal). But that Python throw is std::runtime_error ('CUDA support not compiled'), not the typed DeviceError; that belongs to the bindings slice. Fix: FATAL_ERROR on every explicit ON that cannot be honoured. Gurobi is different: it defaults ON (CMakeLists.txt:472-480 warns 'fall back to HiGHS'), so either default it OFF or treat it as a probe. The fix must also assert DTWC_HAS_CUDA in the linux-cuda-configure job.

Bug 2 (Python conformance never runs): REAL. tests/conformance/test_conformance.py is collected by nothing. python-tests.yml:36 runs `pytest tests/python/`, pyproject.toml has no [tool.pytest] testpaths, tests/CMakeLists.txt registers only the C++ and CLI routes, and the CLAUDE.md runbook also runs only tests/python. The MATLAB twin does run (matlab-mex.yml:55), and so does C++ (macos-unit.yml:59-64). The Python route (and its CLI route, which needs DTWC_CL_PATH) is asserted nowhere. The proposed fix (move the file, or add testpaths) is right; the CLI half needs DTWC_CL_PATH set in CI or a skip-with-reason.

Bug 3 (vacuous cases): PARTLY REAL. test_scores_adversarial.cpp:228-262 is real. Its only unconditional assertion is size==4, and the range checks sit inside if(!isnan), so an NaN silhouette for the a=b=0 case passes. The case also cannot tell whether silhouette returns NaN there, which is an unpinned scores contract that should be settled (0 or a typed NaN) against sklearn. test_arow_adversarial.cpp:619-664 is real: it ends with 'No REQUIRE here — this test always passes', only WARNs, and GT-1 counts per binary, not per case. test_lb_enhanced_webb.cpp:513 is NOT a gate issue: it is tagged \"[.]\" (hidden), so Catch2 does not run it under ctest. It is an unregistered manual bench, so delete or move it to benchmarks, but it does not make the gate green falsely.
Interface notes: From a user's side, this slice is mostly build and CI. The user-facing parts are the CMake options, presets and examples.

(1) CMake options. There are 19 user-facing DTWC_* options: BUILD_{EXAMPLES,TESTING,BENCHMARK,PYTHON,MATLAB}, BENCHMARK_PMU, DEV_MODE, ENABLE_{GUROBI,HIGHS,MPI,CUDA,METAL,ARROW,YAML,LLFIO,NATIVE_ARCH,COVERAGE}, HIGHS_GPU, ALLOW_SEQUENTIAL and REPRODUCIBLE_BUILD. There are also 13 maintainer options, 5 of which are dead (finding 15). DTWC_ENABLE_GUROBI defaults ON and probes (including a C:/gurobi* glob) on every configure; defaulting it OFF would make configure quieter. DTWC_ENABLE_LLFIO is declared inside a function (Dependencies.cmake:192), so it does not appear until dependencies are set up. It disappears entirely if llfio is replaced (finding 14).

(2) The device story (device=cpu|gpu|hpc) is well represented in tests: test_cli_device_matrix drives the real binary across the method × device table, and test_hpc drives the HPC route. But the build layer contradicts the 'no silent fallback' rule for CUDA on macOS (silent bug 1).

(3) Presets. 6 configure presets exist and are pinned by a 219-line configure-time drift check. That check is a tax on adding a preset (for example a linux-cuda or all-off preset that ledger B-15 wants). Delete it (finding 1).

(4) Examples. The C++ examples still use the 1.x-style dtwBanded/ddtwBanded free functions (example_new_features.cpp:40-43), while the Tier-1 surface is dtwc::cluster/load/device. The examples should show the Tier-1 call first. The Python examples are never executed.

(5) The shipped Python package embeds developer SLURM subcommands (`slurm_remote.sh test|benchmark`) that need a repo checkout. Only the device='hpc' submit path belongs in the wheel.

(6) Vocabulary. Test names still carry campaign IDs (F14, F20, D3, Wave2A, Phase0, M47, A15). A user or contributor reading ctest output sees audit codes, not subjects. The target layout is tests/{core,algorithms,mip,io,cli,api}/test_<subject>.cpp.

Minimal test set that still pins every real contract, each with an independent oracle:
- DTW kernels, all variants × L1/SqL2 × banded/full × ndim: the brute full-matrix DP in tests/support/dtw_oracle.hpp plus hand-computed cases (one core/test_dtw.cpp).
- Lower bounds and envelopes: the 2 exhaustive derivation files plus one core/test_lower_bounds.cpp.
- Distance-matrix fill (dense, pruned, mmap, CUDA, Metal, MPI): entrywise equality with pairwise DTW, and NaN = uncomputed. GPU and MPI registered MAY_SKIP with a reason.
- decode_pair: brute enumeration plus MSL parity.
- Clustering (FastPAM/FasterPAM/OneBatch/CLARA/CLARANS/hierarchical/Lloyd/MIP/LR-core/TADPole): cost == brute-force p-median optimum on small N for exact methods, and a bounded gap for heuristics.
- Scores: hand-computed 4-point values in C++, plus sklearn (BSD-3) as the Python oracle for silhouette/ARI/NMI/DB/CH.
- I/O and checkpoints: round-trip bit-exact, corrupt or truncated input rejected, and CSV bytes pinned.
- CLI: the 8 real-binary scripts.
- Cross-language: the conformance reference in C++, Python and MATLAB.
- Compat: the compile probe plus one forwarding table per language.
- Device and typed errors: requests that cannot be honoured throw.

That comes to roughly 45–50k test lines, down from 76k, all oracle-backed. The size is driven by the GPU oracles, the checkpoint robustness corpus and test_hpc.

## Slice records-docs — Process records (.claude/) and documentation (docs/)
Summary: Scope: .claude/ (136 files, ~39.6k lines) and docs/ (72 files, ~19.7k lines), plus README, CHANGELOG and the runbook. The records keep growing and are no longer in step with the code or with each other. A fresh session is told to read about 148 KB (roughly 37k tokens): handoff, CHARTER, MAP, design, PLAN, then ledger rows. MAP.md was taken at 9c08074, and dtwc/python/bindings have since changed in 142 files (+11.5k/-4.1k lines). The newest handoff (09-23) is older than the 09-24 work in PLAN. PLAN §1.1 contradicts the owner's integer rule of 09-27. Most of the volume is one-off material: reports/ is 21.3k lines (13.4k of it the PRIVATE Kasper study), plans/ 3.7k, a 1.2k-line PLAN archive whose stated reason for existing is gone, LESSONS at 1.6k, and a 1.4k-line CHANGELOG entry for a release that was never tagged. In docs/, the main problem is a 1,075-line 'frozen' contract document. Scripts slice it into the user API pages, and a 1,854-line checker enforces it by matching exact sentences, including derivation checks and F-number checks that PLAN §5 dropped. The contract also names 2.0-born names as '1.x', so alias code now protects names nobody ever used. Meanwhile the v1.0.0 CLI flags and v1.0.0 Python camelCase names that people did ship against were removed, and the migration guide says the opposite. The genuine user pages (getting-started, method, guides) are sound. Proposed minimal record set: about 810 lines of required reading.

A check stays only where a third-party API forces a narrower type — mip/index_guard.hpp'. The owner's rule is one compile-time index type, 64-bit by default, a plain cast at 32-bit third-party APIs, and never a panic.   Action: Append the 09-27 instruction verbatim to CHARTER.md. Rewrite PLAN §1.1 and design A19/§9 to say: one compile-time `dtwc::index_t`, int64 by default, for counts and indices, with no count guards. Products stay 64-bit. At 32-bit third-party boundaries: keep the single mip/index_guard.hpp check (a derived N² quantity, silently wrong if truncated), and chunk where we own the loop (MPI per-rank loop, CUDA launches, OpenMP-2.0 loops) instead of casting. Do not delete index_guard.hpp. [MODIFIED by verifier; original: Append the 2026-09-27 instruction verbatim to CHARTER.md. Rewrite PLAN §1.1 and design.md A19/§9: one `dtwc::index_t` (std::int64_t by default), no count guards, plain static_cast at MPI, HiGHS, Gurob]
  Verifier: The record half is right: CHARTER's last entry is 2026-09-23; PLAN §1.1 (45-58) and design.md A19 (line 388) say 'counts are int'; the done items are essays. The integer half is refuted for index_guard and for 'plain static_cast at MPI'. dtwc/mip/index_guard.hpp does not guard a count. It guards N² columns, rows and nonzeros passed to a 32-bit third-party index (HighsInt, the int count of GRBModel::addVars), which overflows at N = 46,341. A plain static_cast there builds a silently wrong model. That violates 'no silent fallback', and the hazard is exactly the third-party-API case the task lists as legitimate. The owner's 09-27 rule concerns counts of series, not derived products crossing a narrower foreign type. Likewise, mpi_distance_matrix.cpp:132-135 already does `static_cast<int>(local_count)` for the MSVC OpenMP 2.0 signed loop, and above INT_MAX pairs per rank that silently computes nothing. PLAN's fix is chunking (PF-4), not a cast. index_guard has 3 callers: mip_Highs.cpp, mip_Gurobi.cpp and pdlp_lp.cpp.

Keep (looks like bloat, earns its place):
- .claude/CHARTER.md (verbatim owner instructions): It is the only first-hand record of what Volkan asked for, and every YAGNI judgement cites it. Append the 2026-09-27 instruction rather than compacting it.

- DECISIONS.md §1 (killed ideas) and §2 (standing rules): These stop sessions from reopening measured dead ends such as x-space LP solving, the parallel swap sweep and Highway. They are cheap to read and high value.
- The mathematics in docs/derivations/02 and 03 (LB_Keogh, LB_Enhanced/Webb admissibility): If the bound is inadmissible, pruning silently gives wrong answers. The proofs are the reason the pruned fill is exact; only the process tables around them are bloat.
- assert_cli_reference in check_docs_contract.py (the live `dtwc_cl --help` compared with cli.md and configuration.md): It is the one docs check derived from the real binary, and it catches drift a user would hit. It should be extended to README and .claude/commands, which have drifted.
- Baselines cited by code, tests, docs or DECISIONS (x27-eigen-gap, d2-lb-keogh, d3-lb-enhanced-webb, r2-d1-dtw, f12-gpu-fixed-band-parity, independent-mv, msm-twe, pdlp-bench, x04/x15/x24/x29, macos-first-baseline): They hold the numbers behind current claims and code comments, as the verification rules require.
- .claude/skills/{dtwc-verify,session-handoff,dtwc-run-benchmarks}: Repeated workflows made into skills, as the runbook's rule 9 asks. Small (about 190 lines).
- .claude/commands/* and .claude/skills/dtwcpp: A user-facing feature documented in README.md:219 and docs/content/getting-started/ai-commands.md. Fix the dead flags (silent_bugs) rather than cut them.
- docs/content user pages (getting-started/*, method/*, guides/devices.md, data-formats, checkpointing, slurm, mpi-cuda-setup): Genuine user documentation. Apart from the process-ID leaks noted, the pages are distinct in scope.
- Hand-kept C++ camelCase shims for names that v1.0.0 shipped (Problem methods, maxIter/N_repetition, DataLoader::startColumn/startRow) and the Python Problem.cluster_size alias: The v1.0.0 surface did ship these (git show v1.0.0:dtwc/Problem.hpp and python/py_main.cpp). 'A break needs a solid reason.'
- docs/examples/quickstart.{py,m} feeding the landing page: Examples that actually run, so the README and landing snippets cannot drift.
- docs/doxygen-awesome*.css (vendored, MIT): A third-party theme pinned in-tree. Fetching it at CI time would add a network dependency for no gain.
Silent bugs reported:
- docs/content/guides/migration.md:115 (generated from docs/api-contract-2.0.md §4); dtwc/cli/config.cpp:240-360; CHANGELOG.md; .claude/PLAN.md §3.7: The migration guide says 'Old flag spellings are accepted with a deprecation warning'. In fact every v1.0.0 CLI flag except --clusters is gone, and neither the break register nor the CHANGELOG records it. Users of the shipped v1.0.0 CLI get an unknown-option error, and the docs tell them the opposite. (`git show v1.0.0:dtwc/dtwc_cl.cpp` lines 43-54 define --Nc, --number_of_clusters, --probName, --in, --out, --skipRows, --skipCols/--skipColumns, --maxIter/--iter, --repeat/--Nrepeat/--Nrepetition/--Nrep, --mip_solver/--mipSolver, --bandwidth/--bandw/--bandlength, --distMat/--distance_matrix/--distances. `grep -c -- '--<flag>\b' dtwc/cli/config.cpp` gives 0 for every one of them. Only '--clusters' is kept, with warn_deprecated (config.cpp:258-260). `git grep -- '--Nc|skipRows|--probName|--bandwidth|--distMat'` over PLAN, DECISIONS, design, CHANGELOG and docs gives 0 hits.)
- docs/api-contract-2.0.md §3 rows 2,7,28a,29a,29b,39,40; docs/content/guides/migration.md; python/src/_dtwcpp_core.cpp: The rename table gives the wrong '1.x' Python names. v1.0.0 Python exposed camelCase methods (set_numberOfClusters, fillDistanceMatrix, assignClusters, calculateMedoids, findTotalCost, distByInd, writeClusters, startColumn, ...). 2.0 drops all of them without a migration note, while it keeps deprecated aliases for 2.0-born names nobody used. (`git show v1.0.0:python/py_main.cpp` binds set_numberOfClusters, fillDistanceMatrix, assignClusters, calculateMedoids, findTotalCost, distByInd, maxDistance, writeClusters, startColumn, startRow and cluster_and_process. `grep -c '"<name>"' python/src/_dtwcpp_core.cpp` gives 0 for each. The contract row 2 lists the 1.x Python name as `set_number_of_clusters`, which has 0 hits in v1.0.0. The failure is loud (AttributeError), but the migration guide misleads. Whether v1.0.0 was ever published to PyPI is unknown.)
- .claude/commands/cluster.md:160; .claude/commands/help.md:105; .claude/commands/troubleshoot.md:77,89; docs/content/getting-started/ai-commands.md:39-49: The AI commands shipped with the repo tell the model to run dtwc_cl with flags that do not exist, so the /cluster fallback and the troubleshoot advice fail. (The commands use --output-dir, --k, --repetitions and --prune lb_keogh. The real flag list (dtwc/cli/config.cpp:240-360) has -o/--output, -k/--n-clusters and --n-init, and no --prune or --repetitions. help.md also tells the model to 'read source files (dtwc/dtwc_cl.cpp)', which is now a 133-line shim over dtwc/cli/run.cpp. The docs checker's CLI sync covers only cli.md and configuration.md (check_docs_contract.py:1795-1825).)
Verifier on bugs: Silent bug 1 (the migration guide claims old CLI flags still work): REAL, but the auditor overstates it. migration.md:115, generated from contract §4, says 'Old flag spellings are accepted with a deprecation warning'. v1.0.0 dtwc_cl.cpp:42-53 defined --Nc, --number_of_clusters, --probName, --in, --out, --skipRows, --skipCols/--skipColumns, --maxIter/--iter, --repeat/--Nrepeat/--Nrepetition/--Nrep, --mip_solver/--mipSolver, --bandwidth/--bandw/--bandlength and --distMat/--distance_matrix/--distances. dtwc/cli/ has none of these (grep count 0 for each). Not everything except --clusters is gone, though: --clusters (with warn_deprecated, config.cpp:257-260), --name, -i/--input, -o/--output, --method and --solver survive. A grep of CHANGELOG, docs and PLAN finds no record of the removals. The failure is loud (a CLI11 unknown-option error); what is wrong is the documentation.

Silent bug 2 (the v1.0.0 Python camelCase names are dropped and mislabelled): REAL, with one correction and one extension. v1.0.0 python/py_main.cpp:23-49 binds set_numberOfClusters, fillDistanceMatrix, assignClusters, calculateMedoids, findTotalCost, distByInd, maxDistance, writeClusters, refreshDistanceMatrix and cluster_and_process. Each has 0 quoted hits in python/src/_dtwcpp_core.cpp, and only cluster_size survives. Contract row 2 gives set_number_of_clusters as the '1.x' name, which has 0 hits in v1.0.0. Correction: startColumn and startRow were commented out in v1.0.0 py_main.cpp:56-63, so Python never shipped them. v1.0.0 did ship setup.py and pyproject.toml; whether it reached PyPI is unknown. Extension: the C++ v1.0.0 public Problem::cluster_by_kMedoidsPAM() was also removed without a shim, while row 27 lists the 2.0-born cluster_by_kMedoidsLloyd as the '1.x' name. That is a compile break for v1.0.0 C++ users, and the docs say nothing about it.

Silent bug 3 (the AI commands use dead dtwc_cl flags): REAL for the files under .claude/commands/. cluster.md:157,160 uses --k and --output-dir; help.md:103,105 uses --k and --output-dir; troubleshoot.md:77,89 uses --repetitions and --prune lb_keogh. The live flags are -k/--n-clusters, -o/--output and --n-init, and --prune does not exist. help.md:15 sends the model to dtwc/dtwc_cl.cpp, now a 133-line shim. The cited docs/content/getting-started/ai-commands.md:39-49 is not a bug site: those lines show slash-command syntax, not dtwc_cl flags.
Interface notes: How it looks to a user today: the docs site has good getting-started and method pages. The API reference (api/tier-1.md, tier-2.md) and the migration guide, however, are cut from an internal governance document. They are full of tags like `[introduced-2.0]`, `[gap F26]`, 'IF-2 S3', 'FX-15' and 'PLAN.md retains F22's evidence verdict ... falsified at 33/46'. The migration guide covers renames of 2.0-born names nobody used. It says nothing about what a real v1.0.0 user must change: every v1.0.0 CLI flag except --clusters, and all v1.0.0 Python camelCase methods, are gone. Worse, it says old CLI flags still work. The CHANGELOG entry for 2.0 is 1,385 lines of internal churn, next to an rc1 heading for an rc that was never tagged.

A nicer surface would have: (1) one short hand-written API page per tier, with the same concept tables across C++, Python, MATLAB and CLI (device=cpu|gpu|hpc first, then load → cluster → Result), and Doxygen for C++ signatures. (2) A migration page that starts from v1.0.0 and covers only what shipped there: the C++ Problem camelCase shims, which stay deprecated for one release; the CLI flags, where the honest options are to restore the handful of v1 spellings as CLI11 aliases with a warning (cheap, and the owner says people use this library) or to list them as removed; and the Python camelCase names, listed as removed. (3) A CHANGELOG 2.0 section of about 150-200 lines. (4) The AI commands kept in step with the real CLI by the single remaining docs gate. There should be no 'frozen contract' layer: conformance tests and Doxygen already pin the surface.

Proposed minimal record set, about 810 lines of required reading, roughly 12-15k tokens and well under 15 minutes:
- CLAUDE.md runbook: 80 lines or fewer.
- Newest handoff: 60 lines or fewer, and only one kept.
- CHARTER.md: about 170 lines, verbatim plus the 09-27 entry.
- MAP.md: 150 lines or fewer, regenerated, no line anchors.
- design.md: 200 lines or fewer, amendments folded in, integer rule updated.
- PLAN.md: 150 lines or fewer, open items only, one or two lines each.

Kept but read on demand:
- DECISIONS.md: 150 lines or fewer (killed ideas and standing rules).
- LESSONS.md: 300 lines or fewer.
- CITATIONS.md: about 300 lines.
- UNIMODULAR.md: the owner's note.
- The baselines that something cites.
- skills/ and commands/.
- plans/if2-config-design.md, until IF-2 closes.

Deleted, with git keeping them: reports/, specs/, the PLAN archive, finished plans, old handoffs, uncited baselines, openmp-crashcourse.md, TODO.md. .claude/reports/ and .claude/plans/ become gitignored scratch.

Net effect of this slice: about 39.6k lines of tracked records and about 20k lines of docs and CHANGELOG shrink by roughly 37k lines.

Also note: the user's auto-memory (outside the repo) still names a repo-root PLAN.md and AGENTS.md. Neither exists: `ls AGENTS.md` fails and only .claude/CLAUDE.md is tracked.
